# The corrected paper and its Lean formalization — tbs_neural_causal_pi

For each result of the corrected paper: its statement, the Lean declaration that states it, whether that declaration is proved, and how the two statements correspond phrase by phrase. A result stated differently from the original paper is marked "corrected"; the report says what changed and why.

## Theorem 3.3

**Statement (corrected).** Given any SCM model $ \mathcal{M} \in \mathcal{M}(\mathcal{G} ,\mathcal{F}_L ,\boldsymbol{U})$, let the treatment variable set be $ \boldsymbol{T} =\{T_k\}_{k=1}^{n_t}$ and suppose that \cref{assump:independence_u}, \ref{assump:categorical_rv} and \ref{assump:Lip_and_bounded} hold for $\mathcal{M}$ with Lipschitz constant $L$, constant $K$. For any intervention $\boldsymbol T=\boldsymbol t$ and $\hat{\mathcal M}\in\mathcal M(\mathcal G,\hat{\mathcal F},\hat{\boldsymbol U})$, we have \begin{equation*} W\left(P^{\mathcal M}(\boldsymbol V(\boldsymbol t)),P^{\hat{\mathcal M}}(\hat{\boldsymbol V}(\boldsymbol t))\right) \leqslant C_{\mathcal G}(L,K;\{d_i,n_i\})\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n_V}\|f_i-\hat f_i\|_\infty+W\left(P^{\mathcal M}(\boldsymbol U),P^{\hat{\mathcal M}}(\hat{\boldsymbol U})\right)\right), \end{equation*} where $C_{\mathcal G}(L,K;\{d_i,n_i\})$ is a constant that only depends on $L,K$, the causal graph $\mathcal G$, the continuous output dimensions $d_i$, and the categorical cardinalities $n_i$, and $f_i,\hat f_i$ are structural functions of $\mathcal M$ and $\hat{\mathcal M}$ respectively.

**Lean.** `Papers.TbsNeuralCausalPi.approximation_wasserstein_bound` — `lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/TApproximation.lean:30`

**Status.** proved in Lean (the corrected statement), under 9 condition(s) made explicit. **Axioms.** the standard ones only (propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound).

**Conditions and readings, and the Lean definitions that carry them.**

- reading: $C$ is a $C^2$-component precisely when it is inclusion maximal among subsets of $\boldsymbol V$ whose distinct vertices are pairwise joined by a bidirected arrow. → `C2ComponentMaximalReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:58`)
- reading: Writing $x=(\text{Pa}(V_i),\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i})$, the displayed law means $P(V_i=k\mid x)=(f_i(x))_k/\|f_i(x)\|_1$ for the nonnegative weight vector defining the categorical distribution, with zero coordinates interpreted using $\log 0=-\infty$. → `CategoricalLawConditionalReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:400`)
- reading: For categorical $V_i$, $f_i(x)=(\mathbb{P}(V_i=j\mid x))_{j\in[n_i]}$ is the structural propensity vector, and $\|f_i(x)-f_i(x')\|\leqslant L_f\|x-x'\|$ on its structural input domain, using the paper's $1$-norm. → `PropensityLipschitzReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:408`)
- reading: There is a deterministic $K>0$ such that $\max_{i\in[n_V],j\in[n_U]}\{\|V_i\|_\infty,\|U_j\|_\infty\}\leqslant K$ almost surely under the SCM law. → `BoundednessAlmostSureReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:415`)
- reading: For distinct observed vertices $V_i,V_j$, the ``correlated latent variable pair'' criterion means that some $U_k\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i}$ and $U_l\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_j}$ are not independent, with $k=l$ permitted. → `BidirectedArrowReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:439`)
- reading: In the categorical branch of (\ref{eq:structure_equation}), zero coordinates of the normalized nonnegative vector $f_i(x)$ are allowed and are evaluated using $\log 0=-\infty$. → `CanonicalZeroPropensityReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:715`)
- reading: Under $\boldsymbol{T}=\boldsymbol{t}$, every unreplaced equation is evaluated in topological order at the intervened values of its parents, with the original latent law unchanged; for canonical categorical nodes the unreplaced equation is the categorical branch of (\ref{eq:structure_equation}). → `InterventionRecursiveEvaluationReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:96`)
- reading: The structural uniform infinity norm takes the supremum of the coordinatewise infinity norm over the common structural input domain. → `StructuralSupNormConvention` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:205`)
- reading: For a categorical propensity coordinate equal to zero, $\log0=-\infty$ in the canonical argmax construction. → `LogZeroConventionApprox` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:172`)
- definition of the paper: (Structural Causal Model) A Structural Causal Model (SCM) is a tuple $ \mathcal{M} =(\boldsymbol{V} ,\boldsymbol{U} , F ,P(\boldsymbol{U}),\mathcal{G}_0)$, where $ \boldsymbol{V} = \{V_i\}_{i=1}^{n_V}$ is the set of observed variables; $ \boldsymbol{U} = \{U_j\}_{j=1}^{n_U}$ is the set of latent variables; $ P(\boldsymbol{U})$ is the distribution of \ latent variables; $\mathcal{G}_0$ is an acyclic directed graph whose nodes are $\boldsymbol{V}$. The values of each observed variable $V_i$ are generated by \begin{equation} V_{i} =f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}) ,\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right) ,\quad \text{where} \ V_{i} \notin \text{Pa}( V_{i}) \ \text{and} \ \boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}} \subset \boldsymbol{U},\label{eq:structure_eq} \end{equation} where $ F = (f_{{1}} ,\cdots,f_{{n_{V}}})$, $\text{Pa}(V)$ is the set of parents of $V$ in graph $\mathcal{G}_0$ and $\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i}$ is the set of latent variables that affect $V_i$. $V_i$ takes either continuous values $\mathbb{R}^{d_i}$ or categorical in $[n_i]$. We extend graph $\mathcal{G}_0$ by adding bi-directed arrows between any $V_i,V_j\in \mathcal{G}_0$ if there exists a correlated latent variable pair $(U_k,U_l), U_k\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i}, U_l\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_j}$. We call the extended graph $\mathcal{G}_\mathcal{M}$ the causal graph of $\mathcal{M}$. → `StructuralSCM` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:157`)
- definition of the paper: A SCM $ \mathcal{M}$ with causal graph $ \mathcal{G}$ has canonical form if \begin{enumerate} \item The set of latent variables consists of two sets, \begin{equation*} \boldsymbol{U} =\left\{U_{C} :C\ \text{is a} \ C^{2}\text{-component of} \ \mathcal{G}\right\} \cup \left\{G_{V_{i}} =( g^{V_{i}}_{1} ,\cdots ,g^{V_{i}}_{n_{i}}) :V_{i} \ \text{is categorical}\right\} , \end{equation*} where $ U_{C}$ and $ g_j^{V_{i}}$ are independent and $ g_j^{V_{i}}$ are standard Gumbel variables. \item The structure equations have the form \begin{equation}\label{eq:structure_equation} V_{i} =\begin{cases} f_{i}\left(\text{Pa} (V_{i} ),\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right) , & V_{i} \ \text{is continuous} ,\\ \arg\max_{k\in [ n_{i}]}\left\{g^{V_{i}}_{k} +\log\left( f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}) ,\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right)\right)_{k}\right\} , & V_{i} \ \text{is categorical} , \|f_i\|_1=1 , \end{cases} \end{equation} where $ \boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}} =\{U_{C} :V_{i} \in C, C \ \text{is a} \ C^{2}\text{-component of} \ \mathcal{G}\}$ and $(x)_k$ is the $k$-th coordinate of the vector $x$. We further assume that $f_i$ are normalized for categorical variables. \end{enumerate} Given a function class $\mathcal{F}$, the SCM class $\mathcal{M} (\mathcal{G} ,\mathcal{F} ,\boldsymbol{U} )$ consists of all canonical SCM models with causal graph $\mathcal{G}$ such that $ f_{i} \in \mathcal{F} ,i\in [ n_{V}]$. → `CanonicalSCM` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:606`)
- definition of the paper: For a causal graph $ \mathcal{G}$, a subset $ C\subset \boldsymbol{V}$ is $ C^{2}$-component if each pair $ V_{i} ,V_{j} \in C$ is connected by a bi-directed arrow in $\mathcal{G}$ and $ C$ is maximal. → `IsC2Component` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:413`)
- definition of the paper: Let $ \boldsymbol{T}\subset \boldsymbol{V}$ be a set of treatment variables. The goal is to estimate causal quantities under a given intervention $ \boldsymbol{T}=\boldsymbol{t}$. Formally, the structural equations of the intervened model are \begin{align*} & T_i = t_i, \quad \forall T_i\in\boldsymbol{T}, \\ & V_{i}(t) =f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}) ,\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right), \quad \forall V_i \notin \boldsymbol{T}. \end{align*} We denote $V_i(\boldsymbol{t})$ to be the value of $V_i$ under the intervention $\boldsymbol{T}=\boldsymbol{t}$ and $P^\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{V}(\boldsymbol{t}))$ to be the distribution of $\boldsymbol{V}(\boldsymbol{t})$ in $\mathcal{M}$. → `interventionalLaw` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:387`)
- definition of the paper: We let $ \mathcal{F}_L(K_1,K_2)$ be the class of Lipschitz $L$-continuous functions $f:K_1\rightarrow K_2$. We may omit the domain and use $\mathcal{F}_L$ when the domain is clear from context. → `LipMap` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:82`)
- definition of the paper: We let $ W(\cdot,\cdot) $ be the Wasserstein-1 distance and $S_\lambda(\mu,\nu)$ be the Sinkhorn distance \cite{chizat2020faster} with regularization parameter $\lambda > 0 $. → `wasserstein1` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:93`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assump:independence_u} All the latent variables in $ \boldsymbol{U}$ are independent. → `LatentBlocksIndependent` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:44`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assump:categorical_rv} The set of latent variables consists of two parts $\boldsymbol{U} =\{U_{1} ,\cdots ,U_{n_{U}} \}\cup \left\{G_{V_{i}} :V_{i} \ \text{is categorical}\right\}$. → `LatentGumbelDecomposition` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:52`)
- condition of the paper: Precisely, if $ V_{i} \in \boldsymbol{V}$ is a categorical variable, the data generation process of $ V_{i}$ satisfies $V_{i} =\arg\max_{k\in [ n_{i}]}\left\{g^{V_i}_{k} +\log\left( f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}),\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right)\right) _{k}\right\}\sim \text{Categorical}(f_i/\|f_i\|_1)$, where $ G_{V_i}= (g^{V_i}_1,\cdots,g^{V_i}_{n_i})$ are i.i.d. standard Gumbel variables, $\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i} \subset \{U_{1} ,\cdots ,U_{n_{U}} \}$. → `CategoricalGumbelArgmax` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:131`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assump:Lip_and_bounded} If $ V_i$ is continuous, $f_i$ in (\ref{eq:structure_equation}) are $L_f$-Lipschitz continuous. → `ContinuousMechanismsLipschitz` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:147`)
- condition of the paper: If $V_i$ is categorical, the propensity functions $f_i(\text{Pa}(V_i), \boldsymbol{U}_{V_i})\triangleq \mathbb{P}(V_i = j|\text{Pa}( V_{i}) ,\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}),j\in[n_i]$ are $L_f$-Lipschitz continuous. → `CategoricalPropensityLipschitz` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:174`)
- condition of the paper: There exists a constant $K>0$ such that $\max_{i\in [n_V],j\in [n_U]}\{\|V_i\|_\infty,\|U_j\|_\infty\} \leqslant K$. → `VariablesBounded` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:191`)
- definition of the paper: We use $ \| \cdotp \|, \|\cdot\|_\infty $ for the $1$-norm and $\infty$-norm and $[n]$ for $\{1,\cdots,n\}$. Bold letters represent sets of random variables. → `L1Vec` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:29`)
- definition of the paper: Let $\Delta_n = \{(p_1,\cdots,p_n):\sum_{i=1}^n p_i=1,p_i\geqslant 0\}$ be the probability simplex. → `probabilitySimplex` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:77`)
- condition of the paper: A model belongs to $\mathcal M(\mathcal G,\mathcal F,\boldsymbol U)$ when it is a canonical SCM whose structural equations use the latent variables only as the $C^2$-components of $\mathcal G$ prescribe, with $f_i\in\mathcal F$; a bi-directed arrow of $\mathcal G$ permits shared latents but does not require them to be correlated, so the latent laws $P(U_C)$ may be arbitrary, degenerate laws included. → `CanonicalClassArbitraryLatentReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:448`)
- condition of the paper: In the categorical structural equations, $\arg\max$ selects the smallest maximizing label when there is a tie. This is a measurable completion on ties; under the stipulated independent standard Gumbel noises and nonnegative propensity weights of positive total, it leaves every fixed-intervention law unchanged. → `CategoricalArgmaxTieSmallestLabel` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:275`)

## Theorem 3.6

**Statement (corrected).** Given any probability measure $\mathbb P$ on $\mathbb R^d$ that satisfies \cref{assump:support}, let $\lambda$ be the Lebesgue measure on $[0,1]^{N_C}$, where $N_C$ is defined in \cref{assump:support}. Write $D_C=\max\{1,\max_i d_i^C\}$. \begin{enumerate} \item There exists a neural network $\hat g\in\mathcal{NN}(W_1,\Theta(D_C),\Theta(d\cdot D_C),L_2,\tau)$ with the above architecture (\cref{fig:wide_nn}) such that \begin{equation*} W(\hat g_{\#}\lambda,\mathbb P)\leqslant O\left(\mathbb I_{\{\max_i d_i^C>0\}}\left(W_1^{-1/D_C}+L_2^{-2/D_C}\right)+(\tau-\tau\log\tau)\right). \end{equation*} \end{enumerate}

The correction states this result with 1 corollary, set out after it.

**Lean.** `Papers.TbsNeuralCausalPi.wide_generator_approximation` — `lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/TWideNn.lean:112`

**Status.** proved in Lean (the corrected statement), given 2 cited result(s), under 17 condition(s) made explicit. **Axioms.** the standard ones only (propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound).

**Conditions and readings, and the Lean definitions that carry them.**

- side condition: For every $k\in[n]$, $\int_{\mathbb R^d}\|x\|\,\mu_k(dx)+\int_{\mathbb R^d}\|x\|\,\hat\mu_k(dx)<\infty$. → `ComponentsFiniteFirstMoment` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Auxiliary.lean:108`)
- reading: A network in $\mathcal{NN}_{k_1,k_2}(W,L)$ has $L$ hidden ReLU layers of width at most $W$, followed by an affine output layer of dimension $k_2$. → `ReLUArchitectureReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:462`)
- reading: The gap event is $E_\delta=\{\exists\,1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n:|G_i+\log p_i-G_j-\log p_j|<\delta\}$, and its union bound counts each unordered pair once. → `GumbelGapEventReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:344`)
- reading: The layer uses $V=[\Vec{v}_1,\ldots,\Vec{v}_{N_C}]$ and the same Gumbel draws for all $\tau>0$, with $\lim_{\tau\downarrow0}\hat{X}^{\tau}_i=\hat{X}^{0}_i=\mathbb{I}_{\{i=\arg\max_{j\in[N_C]}(\log p_j+G_j)\}}$ almost surely for every $i\in[N_C]$. → `GumbelMixingLayerReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:505`)
- reading: In the displayed softmax formula, $X_k$ means $X_k^\tau$, $\mu^\tau=P(X^\tau)$, and $X^0=\lim_{\tau\downarrow0}X^\tau$ almost surely with the same i.i.d. standard Gumbel variables $G_1,\ldots,G_n$ used for every $\tau$. → `GumbelSoftmaxSameDrawReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:578`)
- reading: $f\in\mathcal{H}(K_1,K_2)$ means that $f:K_1\rightarrow K_2$ is bijective and both $f$ and $f^{-1}:K_2\rightarrow K_1$ are continuous. → `HomeomorphismReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:455`)
- reading: For every $k\in[n]$, $\mu_k$ and $\hat\mu_k$ are probability measures on $\mathbb R^d$. → `MixtureComponentsProbability` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Auxiliary.lean:113`)
- reading: The law $\hat g_{\#}\lambda$ includes the independent standard Gumbel variables used in the mixing layer. → `GeneratorLawIncludesGumbel` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:73`)
- reading: For each component of positive dimension $q=d_i^C$, the application of Theorem VIII.1 of \cite{perekrestenkoHighDimensionalDistributionGeneration2022a} uses integer depth parameter $s=1$, quantization parameter $\delta=1/\lceil\sqrt q(q+1)n(n-1)\rceil$, and quantization mesh $\delta/n$. → `PerekrestenkoParameterChoiceReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:594`)
- reading: The error estimates are asymptotic as the network budgets tend to infinity and $\tau\downarrow0$, with constants independent of these budgets and of $\tau$. → `GeneratorBoundAsymptoticReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:85`)
- reading: The measures $\mu$ and $\hat\mu$ are probability measures. → `MeasuresAreProbability` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Auxiliary.lean:18`)
- reading: A component with $d_i^C=0$ is a singleton, and its two deterministic blocks are the unique map to $[0,1]^0$ and a constant affine map to that singleton. → `SingletonComponentBlocks` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:126`)
- reading: $W$ is Wasserstein-1 distance for the ground distance $\|x-y\|$. → `ProductWassersteinOneNormReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:52`)
- reading: The refinement integer is $n=\lfloor(W_1/A_q)^{1/q}\rfloor$ for $W_1\geqslant A_q2^q$, where $A_q\geqslant1$ depends only on $q$ and bounds the clipped network width by $A_qn^q$. → `PerekrestenkoRefinementIntegerReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:608`)
- reading: $S_\lambda(\mu,\nu)=W_{1,\lambda}(\mu,\nu)-\tfrac12W_{1,\lambda}(\mu,\mu)-\tfrac12W_{1,\lambda}(\nu,\nu)$. → `SinkhornDebiasedReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:147`)
- reading: In the proposition entitled ``Deep distribution approximation'', the multiplicative width factors in $\Theta(D_C)$ and $\Theta(dD_C)$ are absolute numerical constants, independent of the measure, chart constants, dimensions, and component count. → `DeepGeneratorAbsoluteWidthReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:784`)
- reading: The uniform infinity norm is $\|F-\hat F\|_\infty=\sup_{x\in K}\max_{j\in[d_2]}|F_j(x)-\hat F_j(x)|$. → `SupNormConventionPushForward` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:66`)
- definition of the paper: We let $ \mathcal{F}_L(K_1,K_2)$ be the class of Lipschitz $L$-continuous functions $f:K_1\rightarrow K_2$. We may omit the domain and use $\mathcal{F}_L$ when the domain is clear from context. → `LipMap` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:82`)
- definition of the paper: Let $ \mathcal{H}(K_1,K_2) $ be the set of homeomorphisms from $K_1$ to $K_2$, i.e., injective and continuous maps in both directions. → `homeoClass` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:67`)
- definition of the paper: Given a measure $ \mu $ on $ \mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}$ and a measurable function $ f:\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\rightarrow \mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}$, the push-forward measure $ f_{\#} \mu $ is defined as $ f_{\#} \mu ( B) =\mu \left( f^{-1}( B)\right)$ for all measurable sets $ B$. → `pushForward` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:72`)
- definition of the paper: We let $ W(\cdot,\cdot) $ be the Wasserstein-1 distance and $S_\lambda(\mu,\nu)$ be the Sinkhorn distance \cite{chizat2020faster} with regularization parameter $\lambda > 0 $. → `wasserstein1` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:93`)
- definition of the paper: We denote $ \mathcal{NN}_{k_1,k_2}(W,L) $ to be the set of ReLu NNs with input dimension $ k_1 $, output dimension $ k_2 $, width $ W $ and depth $ L $. → `ReLUNet` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Networks.lean:57`)
- definition of the paper: In summary, our construction has the form $ \hat{g} =\hat{g}_{3}^{\tau } \circ \hat{g}_{2} \circ \hat{g}_{1} $, where $ \hat{g}_{i} ,i=1,2$ have the separable form $ \left(\hat{g}_{i}^{1}( x_{1}) ,\cdots ,\hat{g}_{i}^{N_{C}}( x_{N_{C}})\right)$ and $ \hat{g}_{1}^{j} \in \mathcal{NN}_{1,d_j^C}( W_{1} , L_1)$, $ \ \hat{g}_{2}^{j} \in \mathcal{NN}_{d_j^C,d}(W_2 ,L_{2}) ,\ j\in [ N_{C}]$; $ \{d_j^C\} $ are the dimension of cubes in \cref{assump:support}. $ \hat{g}_{3}^{\tau }$ is the Gumbel-Softmax layer with temperature parameter $ \tau>0 $. We slightly abuse the notations and denote this class of neural nets by $\mathcal{NN}(W_1,L_1,W_2,L_2,\tau) $. Putting things together, we obtain the following theorem. → `ThreePartGenerator` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Networks.lean:134`)
- definition of the paper: Suppose that the output of $i$-th coordinate in the first two parts is $ \Vec{v}_i $, the Gumbel-softmax layer in the third part (green box) combines different components of the support. In particular, we want to output $ \Vec{v}_i $ with probability $ p_i = \mathbb{P}(C_i) $. Let $ V = [\Vec{v}_1,\cdots,\Vec{v}_i] $, this can be achieved by outputting $ V X $, where $ X = (X_1,\cdots,X_{N_C})^\mathsf{T} $ is a one-hot random vector with $ \mathbb{P}({X}_i = 1) = p_i$. To use backpropagation in training, we use the Gumbel-Softmax trick \cite{Jang2016CategoricalRW} to (approximately) simulate such a random vector, $\hat{X}^\tau_i = \frac{\exp((\log p_i +G_i)/\tau)}{\sum_{k=1}^{N_C}\exp((\log p_k +G_k)/\tau)}, $ where $ \tau>0 $ is the temperature parameter (a hyperparameter) and $ G_i\sim \text{Gumbel}(0,1) $ are i.i.d. standard Gumbel variables. As $\tau \rightarrow 0$, the distribution of $\hat{X}^\tau$ converges almost surely to the categorical distribution \cite[Proposition 1]{maddisonConcreteDistributionContinuous2017}. In particular, when $ \tau = 0$, we denote $\hat{X}_i^0 = \mathbb{I}_{i = \arg\max_j \{\log p_i + G_i\}}$. The output of the last layer is $V\hat{X}^\tau$. → `gumbelSoftmaxMix` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Networks.lean:122`)
- condition of the paper: The support of measure $ \mathbb{P}$ has finite connected components $ C_{1} ,C_{2} \cdots C_{N_C} $, i.e., $ {\text{supp}(\mathbb{P})} =\bigcup _{i=1}^{N_C} C_{i}$ → `SupportFiniteComponents` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:28`)
- condition of the paper: each component $ C_{i}$ satisfies $\mathcal{H}([0,1]^{d^C_i},C_i) \cap \mathcal{F}_L([0,1]^{d^C_i},C_i) \neq \emptyset$ for some $d_i^C \geqslant 0$. → `ComponentsLipschitzHomeomorphic` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:36`)
- definition of the paper: We use $ \| \cdotp \|, \|\cdot\|_\infty $ for the $1$-norm and $\infty$-norm and $[n]$ for $\{1,\cdots,n\}$. Bold letters represent sets of random variables. → `L1Vec` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:29`)
- condition of the paper: A network in $\mathcal{NN}_{k_1,k_2}(W,L)$ has $L$ hidden layers, each of width at most $W$, followed by the affine output layer (width at most $W$ and exactly $W$ give the same class of functions: a narrower layer is padded with zero units). The covering number $\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,S,\|\cdot\|_1)$ is that of the cited source (Anthony and Bartlett): the least size of a subset $C\subseteq S$ such that every point of $S$ lies at $\ell_1$ distance strictly less than $\epsilon$ from some point of $C$. → `ReLUWidthCoveringOwnerReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:471`)

**Taken from the literature: perekrestenkoHighDimensionalDistributionGeneration2022a, Theorem VIII.1.**

What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TbsNeuralCausalPi.PerekrestenkoQuantizedGeneration` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/CitedResults.lean:73`)

```lean
def PerekrestenkoQuantizedGeneration : Sort 0 := -- @realizes s(natural-number source depth fixed by let s := 1 below; distinct from delta and delta / n)
  ∃ c : ℕ, 0 < c ∧ ∀ q : ℕ, 0 < q → ∃ A : ℝ, 1 ≤ A ∧ -- @realizes \Theta(d_i^C)(depth c * q with one multiplier c for every dimension q; the source's depth (s+3)q-s-1 = 4q-2 at s = 1)
    ∀ (d N : ℕ) (P : ProbabilityMeasure (L1Vec d))
      (S : Fin N → Set (L1Vec d)) (dC : Fin N → ℕ) (L : ℝ≥0),
      SupportFiniteComponents (P : Measure _) S →
      ComponentsLipschitzHomeomorphic S dC L →
      ∀ (i : Fin N), dC i = q →
      ∀ f : homeoClass (unitCube (dC i)) (S i), LipschitzWith L f →
      ∃ B : ℝ, 0 < B ∧ ∀ W₁ : ℕ, A * (2 : ℝ)^q ≤ W₁ → -- @realizes W_1(component width budget above the dimension-dependent threshold)
        let n : ℕ := ⌊((W₁ : ℝ) / A) ^ (1 / (q : ℝ))⌋₊ -- @realizes n(refinement floor chosen from W_1 and the dimension-only width factor A)
        let s : ℕ := 1 -- @realizes s(carrier ℕ and fixed value s = 1; source depth, distinct from quantization delta and mesh delta / n)
        let delta : ℝ := (⌈Real.sqrt q * (q + 1) * n * ((n : ℝ) - 1)⌉₊ : ℝ)⁻¹ -- @realizes \delta(prescribed quantization parameter at the pinned refinement)
        2 ≤ n ∧ A * (n : ℝ)^q ≤ W₁ ∧
        ∃ Phi : ReLUNet 1 (dC i) W₁ (c * q), -- @realizes \hat{g}_1^i(quantized component network of width W_1 and depth proportional to its positive dimension)
          (∀ l ∈ Phi.hidden, (l.output : ℝ) ≤ A * (n : ℝ)^q) ∧
          Phi.paramsQuantized (delta / n) ∧
          wasserstein1 ((ProbabilityTheory.cond (P : Measure _) (S i)).map (chartInverse f))
            ((uniformCubeLaw 1).map Phi.eval) ≤
              ENNReal.ofReal (B * (W₁ : ℝ) ^ (-(1 : ℝ) / q))
```

The passage of the proof:

> By \cite[Theorem VIII.1]{perekrestenkoHighDimensionalDistributionGeneration2022a}, there exists quantized ReLu network $ \hat{g}_{1}^{i}$ of width $ W_{1}$ and depth $ \Theta( d_i^C)$ (let $ s=\frac{1}{\lceil n( n-1)\rceil }$ in \cite[Theorem VIII.1]{perekrestenkoHighDimensionalDistributionGeneration2022a}), such that \begin{equation*}
>     W\left(\left( (g_{2}^{i})^{-1}\right)_{\#} \mu _{i} ,P(\hat{g}_{1}^{i}(U_{i}))\right) \leqslant O\left( W_{1}^{-1/d_i^C}\right) ,
>     \end{equation*}
>     where $ U_{i}$ are i.i.d. uniform random variables on $ [ 0,1]$.

**Taken from the literature: Yarotsky2018OptimalAO, Theorem 2.**

What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TbsNeuralCausalPi.YarotskyCoordinateApprox` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/CitedResults.lean:115`)

```lean
def YarotskyCoordinateApprox : Sort 0 :=
  ∀ d : ℕ, 0 < d → ∃ a c : ℝ, 0 < a ∧ 0 < c ∧
    ∀ (D : ℕ) (S : Set (L1Vec D)), IsCompact S →
      ∀ (f : homeoClass (unitCube d) S) (L : ℝ≥0), LipschitzWith L f →
        ∀ j : Fin D, ∀ depth : ℕ, 1 ≤ depth →
          ∃ q : ReLUNet d 1 (12*d) depth,
            ∀ x ∈ unitCube d,
              |(if hx : x ∈ unitCube d then (f ⟨x,hx⟩).val.ofLp j else 0) -
                (q.eval x).ofLp 0| ≤
                a * euclidModulus
                  (fun y => if hy : y ∈ unitCube d then (f ⟨y,hy⟩).val.ofLp j else 0)
                  (c * (depth : ℝ) ^ (-(2 : ℝ) / d))
```

The passage of the proof:

> By \cite[Theorem 2]{Yarotsky2018OptimalAO}, there exist a deep ReLu network $ \hat{g}_{2}^{i,j}$ of width $ \Theta\left( d_{i}^{C}\right)$ and depth $ L_{2}$ such that     
>     \begin{equation*}
>     \| \left(g_{2}^{i}\right)_{j} -\hat{g}_{2}^{i,j} \| _{\infty } \leqslant O\left( L_{2}^{-2/d_{i}^{C}}\right) ,
>     \end{equation*}
>     Let $ \hat{g}_{2}^{i}( x) =\left(\hat{g}_{2}^{i,1}( x) ,\cdots ,\hat{g}_{2}^{i,d}( x)\right)$, we get 
>     \begin{equation*}
>     \|  g_{2}^{i} -\hat{g}_{2}^{i} \|_\infty \leqslant O\left( L_{2}^{-2/d_{i}^{C}}\right) .
>     \end{equation*}

## Corollary 3.6.1

**Statement (a corollary the correction of Theorem 3.6 splits off; the original paper does not state it).** Given any probability measure $\mathbb P$ on $\mathbb R^d$ that satisfies \cref{assump:support}, let $\lambda$ be the Lebesgue measure on $[0,1]^{N_C}$, where $N_C$ is defined in \cref{assump:support}, and write $D_C=\max\{1,\max_i d_i^C\}$. Suppose in addition that $\mathbb P$ constrained to each component satisfies \cref{assumption:lower_bound}. There exists a neural network $\hat g\in\mathcal{NN}(\Theta(D_C),L_1,\Theta(d\cdot D_C),L_2,\tau)$ with the architecture of \cref{fig:wide_nn} such that \[ W(\hat g_{\#}\lambda,\mathbb P)\leqslant O\left(\mathbb I_{\{\max_i d_i^C>0\}}\left(L_1^{-2/D_C}+L_2^{-2/D_C}\right)+(\tau-\tau\log\tau)\right). \]

- side condition: For every $k\in[n]$, $\int_{\mathbb R^d}\|x\|\,\mu_k(dx)+\int_{\mathbb R^d}\|x\|\,\hat\mu_k(dx)<\infty$. → `ComponentsFiniteFirstMoment` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Auxiliary.lean:108`)
- reading: A network in $\mathcal{NN}_{k_1,k_2}(W,L)$ has $L$ hidden ReLU layers of width at most $W$, followed by an affine output layer of dimension $k_2$. → `ReLUArchitectureReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:462`)
- reading: The gap event is $E_\delta=\{\exists\,1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n:|G_i+\log p_i-G_j-\log p_j|<\delta\}$, and its union bound counts each unordered pair once. → `GumbelGapEventReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:344`)
- reading: The layer uses $V=[\Vec{v}_1,\ldots,\Vec{v}_{N_C}]$ and the same Gumbel draws for all $\tau>0$, with $\lim_{\tau\downarrow0}\hat{X}^{\tau}_i=\hat{X}^{0}_i=\mathbb{I}_{\{i=\arg\max_{j\in[N_C]}(\log p_j+G_j)\}}$ almost surely for every $i\in[N_C]$. → `GumbelMixingLayerReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:505`)
- reading: In the displayed softmax formula, $X_k$ means $X_k^\tau$, $\mu^\tau=P(X^\tau)$, and $X^0=\lim_{\tau\downarrow0}X^\tau$ almost surely with the same i.i.d. standard Gumbel variables $G_1,\ldots,G_n$ used for every $\tau$. → `GumbelSoftmaxSameDrawReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:578`)
- reading: $f\in\mathcal{H}(K_1,K_2)$ means that $f:K_1\rightarrow K_2$ is bijective and both $f$ and $f^{-1}:K_2\rightarrow K_1$ are continuous. → `HomeomorphismReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:455`)

- condition of the paper: \label{assumption:lower_bound} Suppose that $ \mathbb{P}$ is supported on a compact set $ K\subset \mathbb{R}^{D}$, there exists a constant $ C_{f} >0$, $ f\in \mathcal{H}([0,1]^{d},K) \cap \mathcal{F}_L([0,1]^{d},K)$, such that for any measurable set $ B\subset [ 0,1]^{d}$, $ \mathbb{P}\left( f( B)\right) \geqslant C_{f} \lambda ( B)$. → `LowerBoundDomination` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:59`)
- condition of the paper: Besides, if $d>0$, $(f^{-1})_{\#}\mathbb P$ vanishes on the boundary $\partial[0,1]^d$. → `PullbackVanishesOnBoundary` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:76`)
- definition of the paper: In summary, our construction has the form $ \hat{g} =\hat{g}_{3}^{\tau } \circ \hat{g}_{2} \circ \hat{g}_{1} $, where $ \hat{g}_{i} ,i=1,2$ have the separable form $ \left(\hat{g}_{i}^{1}( x_{1}) ,\cdots ,\hat{g}_{i}^{N_{C}}( x_{N_{C}})\right)$ and $ \hat{g}_{1}^{j} \in \mathcal{NN}_{1,d_j^C}( W_{1} , L_1)$, $ \ \hat{g}_{2}^{j} \in \mathcal{NN}_{d_j^C,d}(W_2 ,L_{2}) ,\ j\in [ N_{C}]$; $ \{d_j^C\} $ are the dimension of cubes in \cref{assump:support}. $ \hat{g}_{3}^{\tau }$ is the Gumbel-Softmax layer with temperature parameter $ \tau>0 $. We slightly abuse the notations and denote this class of neural nets by $\mathcal{NN}(W_1,L_1,W_2,L_2,\tau) $. Putting things together, we obtain the following theorem. → `ThreePartGenerator` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Networks.lean:134`)
- definition of the paper: Suppose that the output of $i$-th coordinate in the first two parts is $ \Vec{v}_i $, the Gumbel-softmax layer in the third part (green box) combines different components of the support. In particular, we want to output $ \Vec{v}_i $ with probability $ p_i = \mathbb{P}(C_i) $. Let $ V = [\Vec{v}_1,\cdots,\Vec{v}_i] $, this can be achieved by outputting $ V X $, where $ X = (X_1,\cdots,X_{N_C})^\mathsf{T} $ is a one-hot random vector with $ \mathbb{P}({X}_i = 1) = p_i$. To use backpropagation in training, we use the Gumbel-Softmax trick \cite{Jang2016CategoricalRW} to (approximately) simulate such a random vector, $\hat{X}^\tau_i = \frac{\exp((\log p_i +G_i)/\tau)}{\sum_{k=1}^{N_C}\exp((\log p_k +G_k)/\tau)}, $ where $ \tau>0 $ is the temperature parameter (a hyperparameter) and $ G_i\sim \text{Gumbel}(0,1) $ are i.i.d. standard Gumbel variables. As $\tau \rightarrow 0$, the distribution of $\hat{X}^\tau$ converges almost surely to the categorical distribution \cite[Proposition 1]{maddisonConcreteDistributionContinuous2017}. In particular, when $ \tau = 0$, we denote $\hat{X}_i^0 = \mathbb{I}_{i = \arg\max_j \{\log p_i + G_i\}}$. The output of the last layer is $V\hat{X}^\tau$. → `gumbelSoftmaxMix` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Networks.lean:122`)
- definition of the paper: Given a measure $ \mu $ on $ \mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}$ and a measurable function $ f:\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\rightarrow \mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}$, the push-forward measure $ f_{\#} \mu $ is defined as $ f_{\#} \mu ( B) =\mu \left( f^{-1}( B)\right)$ for all measurable sets $ B$. → `pushForward` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:72`)
- definition of the paper: We let $ W(\cdot,\cdot) $ be the Wasserstein-1 distance and $S_\lambda(\mu,\nu)$ be the Sinkhorn distance \cite{chizat2020faster} with regularization parameter $\lambda > 0 $. → `wasserstein1` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:93`)
- definition of the paper: We denote $ \mathcal{NN}_{k_1,k_2}(W,L) $ to be the set of ReLu NNs with input dimension $ k_1 $, output dimension $ k_2 $, width $ W $ and depth $ L $. → `ReLUNet` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Networks.lean:57`)

## Corollary 3.8

**Statement (corrected).** Given a causal model $\mathcal M^*\in\mathcal M(\mathcal G,\mathcal F_L,\boldsymbol U)$, suppose that Assumptions \ref{assump:independence_u}-\ref{assump:Lip_and_bounded} hold and the distributions of $U_C$ for all $C^2$-component satisfy the assumptions of the proposition entitled ``Deep distribution approximation''. Let $d_{\max}^{\text{in}}$ and $d_{\max}^{\text{out}}$ be the largest input and output dimension of $f_i$ in (\ref{eq:structure_equation}) and $d_{\max}^U$ be the largest dimension of all latent variables. Suppose in addition that $d_{\max}^{\text{in}}>0$ and $d_{\max}^U>0$. Fix any $R>0$. There exists a neural causal model $$\hat{\mathcal{M}} \in \text{NCM}_{\mathcal{G}} ( \mathcal{NN}(\Theta( d^{\text{in}}_{\max}d^{\text{out}}_{\max}) ,L_{0}) , \mathcal{NN}(\Theta(d_{\max}^U),L_1,\Theta((d_{\max}^U)^2),L_2))$$ with structure equations (\ref{eq:structure_equation_hat}). For any intervention $\boldsymbol{T}=\boldsymbol{t}$ such that $\|t_i\|_\infty\leqslant R$ for each continuous treatment variable $T_i$, $\hat{\mathcal M}$ satisfies \begin{equation*} W\left(P^{\mathcal M^*}(\boldsymbol V(\boldsymbol t)),P^{\hat{\mathcal M}}(\boldsymbol V(\boldsymbol t))\right)\leqslant O\left(L_0^{-2/d_{\max}^{\text{in}}}+L_1^{-2/d_{\max}^U}+L_2^{-2/d_{\max}^U}+(\tau-\tau\log\tau)\right). \end{equation*} The implicit constant is independent of the intervention and the network budgets, but may depend on $R$ and the fixed causal model.

**Lean.** `Papers.TbsNeuralCausalPi.deep_ncm_approximation` — `lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/TDeepNn.lean:303`

**Status.** proved in Lean (the corrected statement), given 3 cited result(s), under 45 condition(s) made explicit. **Axioms.** the standard ones only (propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound).

**Conditions and readings, and the Lean definitions that carry them.**

- side condition: For every $k\in[n]$, $\int_{\mathbb R^d}\|x\|\,\mu_k(dx)+\int_{\mathbb R^d}\|x\|\,\hat\mu_k(dx)<\infty$. → `ComponentsFiniteFirstMoment` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Auxiliary.lean:108`)
- reading: $C$ is a $C^2$-component precisely when it is inclusion maximal among subsets of $\boldsymbol V$ whose distinct vertices are pairwise joined by a bidirected arrow. → `C2ComponentMaximalReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:58`)
- reading: Writing $x=(\text{Pa}(V_i),\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i})$, the displayed law means $P(V_i=k\mid x)=(f_i(x))_k/\|f_i(x)\|_1$ for the nonnegative weight vector defining the categorical distribution, with zero coordinates interpreted using $\log 0=-\infty$. → `CategoricalLawConditionalReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:400`)
- reading: For categorical $V_i$, $f_i(x)=(\mathbb{P}(V_i=j\mid x))_{j\in[n_i]}$ is the structural propensity vector, and $\|f_i(x)-f_i(x')\|\leqslant L_f\|x-x'\|$ on its structural input domain, using the paper's $1$-norm. → `PropensityLipschitzReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:408`)
- reading: There is a deterministic $K>0$ such that $\max_{i\in[n_V],j\in[n_U]}\{\|V_i\|_\infty,\|U_j\|_\infty\}\leqslant K$ almost surely under the SCM law. → `BoundednessAlmostSureReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:415`)
- reading: The phrase ``uniform cubes'' denotes the unit cube domains $[0,1]^m$ used for deterministic uniform function approximation; it imposes no uniform probability law on structural inputs. → `UnitCubeDomainReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:430`)
- reading: For distinct observed vertices $V_i,V_j$, the ``correlated latent variable pair'' criterion means that some $U_k\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i}$ and $U_l\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_j}$ are not independent, with $k=l$ permitted. → `BidirectedArrowReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:439`)
- reading: In the categorical branch of (\ref{eq:structure_equation}), zero coordinates of the normalized nonnegative vector $f_i(x)$ are allowed and are evaluated using $\log 0=-\infty$. → `CanonicalZeroPropensityReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:715`)
- reading: Under $\boldsymbol{T}=\boldsymbol{t}$, every unreplaced equation is evaluated in topological order at the intervened values of its parents, with the original latent law unchanged; for canonical categorical nodes the unreplaced equation is the categorical branch of (\ref{eq:structure_equation}). → `InterventionRecursiveEvaluationReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:96`)
- reading: A network in $\mathcal{NN}_{k_1,k_2}(W,L)$ has $L$ hidden ReLU layers of width at most $W$, followed by an affine output layer of dimension $k_2$. → `ReLUArchitectureReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:462`)
- reading: Membership in $\text{NCM}_{\mathcal G}(\mathcal F_0,\mathcal F_1)$ requires $\hat f_i\in\mathcal F_0$ for every observed node and $\hat g_j\in\mathcal F_1$ for every latent component, with the compatible input and output dimensions understood separately for each network. → `NCMMembershipReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:521`)
- reading: $d_{\max}^U=\max_{j\in[n_U]}d_j^U$ ranges over non-Gumbel latent variables, while $d_{\max}^{\mathrm{in}}=\max_i d_i^{\mathrm{in}}$ and $d_{\max}^{\mathrm{out}}=\max_i d_i^{\mathrm{out}}$, with $d_i^{\mathrm{out}}=n_i$ for categorical nodes. → `LatentDimMaxReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:560`)
- reading: The gap event is $E_\delta=\{\exists\,1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n:|G_i+\log p_i-G_j-\log p_j|<\delta\}$, and its union bound counts each unordered pair once. → `GumbelGapEventReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:344`)
- reading: For each categorical $V_i$, the noise in equation (\ref{eq:structure_equation_hat}) satisfies $g_k=g_k^{V_i}$ for $k\in[n_i]$. → `NCMCategoricalNoiseReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:494`)
- reading: The layer uses $V=[\Vec{v}_1,\ldots,\Vec{v}_{N_C}]$ and the same Gumbel draws for all $\tau>0$, with $\lim_{\tau\downarrow0}\hat{X}^{\tau}_i=\hat{X}^{0}_i=\mathbb{I}_{\{i=\arg\max_{j\in[N_C]}(\log p_j+G_j)\}}$ almost surely for every $i\in[N_C]$. → `GumbelMixingLayerReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:505`)
- reading: In the displayed softmax formula, $X_k$ means $X_k^\tau$, $\mu^\tau=P(X^\tau)$, and $X^0=\lim_{\tau\downarrow0}X^\tau$ almost surely with the same i.i.d. standard Gumbel variables $G_1,\ldots,G_n$ used for every $\tau$. → `GumbelSoftmaxSameDrawReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:578`)
- reading: $f\in\mathcal{H}(K_1,K_2)$ means that $f:K_1\rightarrow K_2$ is bijective and both $f$ and $f^{-1}:K_2\rightarrow K_1$ are continuous. → `HomeomorphismReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:455`)
- reading: For every $k\in[n]$, $\mu_k$ and $\hat\mu_k$ are probability measures on $\mathbb R^d$. → `MixtureComponentsProbability` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Auxiliary.lean:113`)
- reading: The law $\hat g_{\#}\lambda$ includes the independent standard Gumbel variables used in the mixing layer. → `GeneratorLawIncludesGumbel` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:73`)
- reading: The assertion about adjacent cubes is quantified over $i$ satisfying $1\leqslant i<2^{nd}$. → `HilbertAdjacencyRange` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:198`)
- reading: The mass assigned to $I_{O_n(i)}$ is $\lambda_d(C_{O_n(i)})=2^{-nd}$. → `IntervalMassLebesgueReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:332`)
- reading: In the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:holder_curve}, $\mathcal C_n$ denotes the standard dyadic collection $\left\{\prod_{r=1}^d[a_r2^{-n},(a_r+1)2^{-n}]:a_r\in\{0,\ldots,2^n-1\}\right\}$. → `HolderCurveDyadicCellsReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:310`)
- reading: The multiplicative width factors in $\Theta(d_{\max}^{\text{in}}d_{\max}^{\text{out}})$, $\Theta(d_{\max}^U)$, and $\Theta((d_{\max}^U)^2)$ can be chosen as absolute numerical constants, independent of the causal model, $R$, all dimensions, and all category and component counts. → `DeepNCMAbsoluteWidthReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:738`)
- reading: For each latent law $P(U_C)$, the assumptions of the proposition entitled ``Deep distribution approximation'' require the support charts of \cref{assump:support} and \cref{assumption:lower_bound} on each normalized component law $\mu_i=P(U_C)|_{C_i}/P(U_C\in C_i)$, with $(f^{-1})_{\#}\mu_i(\partial[0,1]^{d_i^C})=0$ when $d_i^C>0$. → `DeepNNLatentLawPremisesReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:750`)
- reading: The structural uniform infinity norm takes the supremum of the coordinatewise infinity norm over the common structural input domain. → `StructuralSupNormConvention` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:205`)
- reading: For each component of positive dimension $q=d_i^C$, the application of Theorem VIII.1 of \cite{perekrestenkoHighDimensionalDistributionGeneration2022a} uses integer depth parameter $s=1$, quantization parameter $\delta=1/\lceil\sqrt q(q+1)n(n-1)\rceil$, and quantization mesh $\delta/n$. → `PerekrestenkoParameterChoiceReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:594`)
- reading: The error estimates are asymptotic as the network budgets tend to infinity and $\tau\downarrow0$, with constants independent of these budgets and of $\tau$. → `GeneratorBoundAsymptoticReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:85`)
- reading: The measures $\mu$ and $\hat\mu$ are probability measures. → `MeasuresAreProbability` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Auxiliary.lean:18`)
- reading: In Corollary~\ref{corollary:deep_nn}, for $m_i=d_i^{\text{in}}>0$, the symbol $\hat f_i$ denotes $q_i\circ\Pi_i$ for a continuous variable and the vector $\bigl(\max\{\epsilon_i,(q_i\circ\Pi_i)_k\}\bigr)_{k=1}^{n_i}$ for a categorical variable, where $q_i$, $\Pi_i$, and $\epsilon_i=A_iL_0^{-2/m_i}$ are chosen in its proof. → `ProjectedFlooredApproximantReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:630`)
- reading: In the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:holder_curve}, $\mathcal I_n$ denotes $\{I_{O_n(i)}:1\leqslant i\leqslant2^{nd}\}$ with $I_{O_n(i)}=[(i-1)2^{-nd},i2^{-nd}]$. → `HolderCurveIntervalsReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:324`)
- reading: The latent generator class in the statement uses the same temperature $\tau$ as the displayed error estimate. → `DeepNNSharedTemperatureReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:662`)
- reading: For every $n$, the cells of $\mathcal C_n$ are closed coordinate boxes covering $[0,1]^d$; a cell may have zero side lengths. → `PartitionClosedBoxCellsReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:766`)
- reading: The error estimate is asymptotic as $L_0,L_1,L_2\to\infty$ and $\tau\downarrow0$, for fixed $R$ and fixed causal model. → `DeepNNBoundAsymptoticReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:113`)
- reading: For distinct cells $C,C'\in\mathcal C_n$, the partition condition is $\operatorname{int}(C)\cap\operatorname{int}(C')=\emptyset$, with interiors taken in $\mathbb R^d$. → `PartitionInteriorsDisjointReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:775`)
- reading: A component with $d_i^C=0$ is a singleton, and its two deterministic blocks are the unique map to $[0,1]^0$ and a constant affine map to that singleton. → `SingletonComponentBlocks` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:126`)
- reading: For a categorical propensity coordinate equal to zero, $\log0=-\infty$ in the canonical argmax construction. → `LogZeroConventionApprox` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:172`)
- reading: $W$ is Wasserstein-1 distance for the ground distance $\|x-y\|$. → `ProductWassersteinOneNormReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:52`)
- reading: In Corollary~\ref{corollary:deep_nn}, for $m_i=0$, the symbol $\hat f_i$ denotes the exact constant function $f_i$. → `ZeroInputExactApproximantReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:651`)
- reading: The refinement integer is $n=\lfloor(W_1/A_q)^{1/q}\rfloor$ for $W_1\geqslant A_q2^q$, where $A_q\geqslant1$ depends only on $q$ and bounds the clipped network width by $A_qn^q$. → `PerekrestenkoRefinementIntegerReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:608`)
- reading: The reference measure $\lambda_d$ denotes Lebesgue measure on $[0,1]^d$. → `CubeLebesgueReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:317`)
- reading: Each $O_n$ is a bijection. → `HilbertOrderingBijective` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:191`)
- reading: $S_\lambda(\mu,\nu)=W_{1,\lambda}(\mu,\nu)-\tfrac12W_{1,\lambda}(\mu,\mu)-\tfrac12W_{1,\lambda}(\nu,\nu)$. → `SinkhornDebiasedReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:147`)
- reading: In the proposition entitled ``Deep distribution approximation'', the multiplicative width factors in $\Theta(D_C)$ and $\Theta(dD_C)$ are absolute numerical constants, independent of the measure, chart constants, dimensions, and component count. → `DeepGeneratorAbsoluteWidthReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:784`)
- reading: The cells of $\mathcal C_n$ are closed and may share boundaries. → `HilbertCellsClosed` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:184`)
- reading: The uniform infinity norm is $\|F-\hat F\|_\infty=\sup_{x\in K}\max_{j\in[d_2]}|F_j(x)-\hat F_j(x)|$. → `SupNormConventionPushForward` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:66`)
- definition of the paper: (Structural Causal Model) A Structural Causal Model (SCM) is a tuple $ \mathcal{M} =(\boldsymbol{V} ,\boldsymbol{U} , F ,P(\boldsymbol{U}),\mathcal{G}_0)$, where $ \boldsymbol{V} = \{V_i\}_{i=1}^{n_V}$ is the set of observed variables; $ \boldsymbol{U} = \{U_j\}_{j=1}^{n_U}$ is the set of latent variables; $ P(\boldsymbol{U})$ is the distribution of \ latent variables; $\mathcal{G}_0$ is an acyclic directed graph whose nodes are $\boldsymbol{V}$. The values of each observed variable $V_i$ are generated by \begin{equation} V_{i} =f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}) ,\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right) ,\quad \text{where} \ V_{i} \notin \text{Pa}( V_{i}) \ \text{and} \ \boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}} \subset \boldsymbol{U},\label{eq:structure_eq} \end{equation} where $ F = (f_{{1}} ,\cdots,f_{{n_{V}}})$, $\text{Pa}(V)$ is the set of parents of $V$ in graph $\mathcal{G}_0$ and $\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i}$ is the set of latent variables that affect $V_i$. $V_i$ takes either continuous values $\mathbb{R}^{d_i}$ or categorical in $[n_i]$. We extend graph $\mathcal{G}_0$ by adding bi-directed arrows between any $V_i,V_j\in \mathcal{G}_0$ if there exists a correlated latent variable pair $(U_k,U_l), U_k\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i}, U_l\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_j}$. We call the extended graph $\mathcal{G}_\mathcal{M}$ the causal graph of $\mathcal{M}$. → `StructuralSCM` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:157`)
- definition of the paper: A SCM $ \mathcal{M}$ with causal graph $ \mathcal{G}$ has canonical form if \begin{enumerate} \item The set of latent variables consists of two sets, \begin{equation*} \boldsymbol{U} =\left\{U_{C} :C\ \text{is a} \ C^{2}\text{-component of} \ \mathcal{G}\right\} \cup \left\{G_{V_{i}} =( g^{V_{i}}_{1} ,\cdots ,g^{V_{i}}_{n_{i}}) :V_{i} \ \text{is categorical}\right\} , \end{equation*} where $ U_{C}$ and $ g_j^{V_{i}}$ are independent and $ g_j^{V_{i}}$ are standard Gumbel variables. \item The structure equations have the form \begin{equation}\label{eq:structure_equation} V_{i} =\begin{cases} f_{i}\left(\text{Pa} (V_{i} ),\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right) , & V_{i} \ \text{is continuous} ,\\ \arg\max_{k\in [ n_{i}]}\left\{g^{V_{i}}_{k} +\log\left( f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}) ,\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right)\right)_{k}\right\} , & V_{i} \ \text{is categorical} , \|f_i\|_1=1 , \end{cases} \end{equation} where $ \boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}} =\{U_{C} :V_{i} \in C, C \ \text{is a} \ C^{2}\text{-component of} \ \mathcal{G}\}$ and $(x)_k$ is the $k$-th coordinate of the vector $x$. We further assume that $f_i$ are normalized for categorical variables. \end{enumerate} Given a function class $\mathcal{F}$, the SCM class $\mathcal{M} (\mathcal{G} ,\mathcal{F} ,\boldsymbol{U} )$ consists of all canonical SCM models with causal graph $\mathcal{G}$ such that $ f_{i} \in \mathcal{F} ,i\in [ n_{V}]$. → `CanonicalSCM` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:606`)
- definition of the paper: For a causal graph $ \mathcal{G}$, a subset $ C\subset \boldsymbol{V}$ is $ C^{2}$-component if each pair $ V_{i} ,V_{j} \in C$ is connected by a bi-directed arrow in $\mathcal{G}$ and $ C$ is maximal. → `IsC2Component` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:413`)
- definition of the paper: (Neural Causal Model) A Neural Causal Model (NCM) is a special kind of SCM where $\boldsymbol U=\{U_1,\ldots,U_{n_U}\}\cup\{G_{V_i}:V_i\ \text{categorical}\}$, the scalar coordinates of all the $U_i$ are mutually independent with common $\operatorname{Unif}([0,1])$ law, and $U_i$ groups the indicated finite number $d_i^U$ of these coordinates, so that $U_i\sim\operatorname{Unif}([0,1]^{d_i^U})$. All coordinates of the $G_{V_i}$ are independent standard Gumbel variables, independent of all uniform coordinates. The continuous structural maps and the categorical propensity-weight maps $f_i$ in (\ref{eq:structure_eq}) are Neural Networks (NNs); categorical values are generated from these weights by the Gumbel argmax rule. The weights are nonnegative and have strictly positive sum, with $\log0=-\infty$. → `NeuralCausalModel` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Networks.lean:403`)
- definition of the paper: We let $ \mathcal{F}_L(K_1,K_2)$ be the class of Lipschitz $L$-continuous functions $f:K_1\rightarrow K_2$. We may omit the domain and use $\mathcal{F}_L$ when the domain is clear from context. → `LipMap` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:82`)
- definition of the paper: First, we approximate the functions $ f_i $ in (\ref{eq:structure_equation}) by NNs $ \hat{f}_i $. Then, we approximate the distribution of latent variables by pushing forward uniform and Gumbel variables using neural networks, i.e., $ \hat{U}_{C_j}=\hat{g}_j(Z_{C_j}) $, where $\{C_j\}$ are $C^2$ components and $Z_{C_j}$ are multi-variate uniform and Gumbel random variables. The structural equations of the resulting approximated model $ \hat{\mathcal{M}} $ are \begin{equation}\label{eq:structure_equation_hat} \hat{V}_{i} =\begin{cases} \hat{f}_{i}\left(\text{Pa} (\hat{V}_{i} ),(\hat{g}_{j} (Z_{C_{j}} ))_{U_{C_{j} }\in \boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}}\right), & V_{i} \text{ is continuous} ,\\ \arg\max_{k\in [ n_{i}]}\left\{g_{k} +\log\left(\hat{f}_{i}\left(\text{Pa} (\hat{V}_{i} ),(\hat{g}_{j} (Z_{C_{j}} ))_{U_{C_{j} } \in \boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}}\right) \right)_{k}\right\} , & V_{i} \text{ is categorical}. \end{cases} \end{equation} → `ncmEval` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Networks.lean:566`)
- definition of the paper: Let $ \text{NCM}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{F}_0,\mathcal{F}_1)$ be a collection of NCMs with structural equation (\ref{eq:structure_equation_hat}) and $ \hat{f}_{i} \in \mathcal{F}_0, \hat{g} \in \mathcal{F}_1. $ → `NCMIn` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Networks.lean:510`)
- definition of the paper: We denote $ \mathcal{NN}_{k_1,k_2}(W,L) $ to be the set of ReLu NNs with input dimension $ k_1 $, output dimension $ k_2 $, width $ W $ and depth $ L $. → `ReLUNet` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Networks.lean:57`)
- definition of the paper: In summary, our construction has the form $ \hat{g} =\hat{g}_{3}^{\tau } \circ \hat{g}_{2} \circ \hat{g}_{1} $, where $ \hat{g}_{i} ,i=1,2$ have the separable form $ \left(\hat{g}_{i}^{1}( x_{1}) ,\cdots ,\hat{g}_{i}^{N_{C}}( x_{N_{C}})\right)$ and $ \hat{g}_{1}^{j} \in \mathcal{NN}_{1,d_j^C}( W_{1} , L_1)$, $ \ \hat{g}_{2}^{j} \in \mathcal{NN}_{d_j^C,d}(W_2 ,L_{2}) ,\ j\in [ N_{C}]$; $ \{d_j^C\} $ are the dimension of cubes in \cref{assump:support}. $ \hat{g}_{3}^{\tau }$ is the Gumbel-Softmax layer with temperature parameter $ \tau>0 $. We slightly abuse the notations and denote this class of neural nets by $\mathcal{NN}(W_1,L_1,W_2,L_2,\tau) $. Putting things together, we obtain the following theorem. → `ThreePartGenerator` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Networks.lean:134`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assump:independence_u} All the latent variables in $ \boldsymbol{U}$ are independent. → `LatentBlocksIndependent` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:44`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assump:categorical_rv} The set of latent variables consists of two parts $\boldsymbol{U} =\{U_{1} ,\cdots ,U_{n_{U}} \}\cup \left\{G_{V_{i}} :V_{i} \ \text{is categorical}\right\}$. → `LatentGumbelDecomposition` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:52`)
- condition of the paper: Precisely, if $ V_{i} \in \boldsymbol{V}$ is a categorical variable, the data generation process of $ V_{i}$ satisfies $V_{i} =\arg\max_{k\in [ n_{i}]}\left\{g^{V_i}_{k} +\log\left( f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}),\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right)\right) _{k}\right\}\sim \text{Categorical}(f_i/\|f_i\|_1)$, where $ G_{V_i}= (g^{V_i}_1,\cdots,g^{V_i}_{n_i})$ are i.i.d. standard Gumbel variables, $\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i} \subset \{U_{1} ,\cdots ,U_{n_{U}} \}$. → `CategoricalGumbelArgmax` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:131`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assump:Lip_and_bounded} If $ V_i$ is continuous, $f_i$ in (\ref{eq:structure_equation}) are $L_f$-Lipschitz continuous. → `ContinuousMechanismsLipschitz` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:147`)
- condition of the paper: If $V_i$ is categorical, the propensity functions $f_i(\text{Pa}(V_i), \boldsymbol{U}_{V_i})\triangleq \mathbb{P}(V_i = j|\text{Pa}( V_{i}) ,\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}),j\in[n_i]$ are $L_f$-Lipschitz continuous. → `CategoricalPropensityLipschitz` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:174`)
- condition of the paper: There exists a constant $K>0$ such that $\max_{i\in [n_V],j\in [n_U]}\{\|V_i\|_\infty,\|U_j\|_\infty\} \leqslant K$. → `VariablesBounded` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:191`)
- condition of the paper: The support of measure $ \mathbb{P}$ has finite connected components $ C_{1} ,C_{2} \cdots C_{N_C} $, i.e., $ {\text{supp}(\mathbb{P})} =\bigcup _{i=1}^{N_C} C_{i}$ → `SupportFiniteComponents` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:28`)
- condition of the paper: each component $ C_{i}$ satisfies $\mathcal{H}([0,1]^{d^C_i},C_i) \cap \mathcal{F}_L([0,1]^{d^C_i},C_i) \neq \emptyset$ for some $d_i^C \geqslant 0$. → `ComponentsLipschitzHomeomorphic` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:36`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assumption:lower_bound} Suppose that $ \mathbb{P}$ is supported on a compact set $ K\subset \mathbb{R}^{D}$, there exists a constant $ C_{f} >0$, $ f\in \mathcal{H}([0,1]^{d},K) \cap \mathcal{F}_L([0,1]^{d},K)$, such that for any measurable set $ B\subset [ 0,1]^{d}$, $ \mathbb{P}\left( f( B)\right) \geqslant C_{f} \lambda ( B)$. → `LowerBoundDomination` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:59`)
- condition of the paper: Besides, if $d>0$, $(f^{-1})_{\#}\mathbb P$ vanishes on the boundary $\partial[0,1]^d$. → `PullbackVanishesOnBoundary` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:76`)
- condition of the paper: For simplicity, we assume that the domain of the functions are uniform cubes. → `StructuralDomainsUnitCubes` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:216`)
- condition of the paper: A model belongs to $\mathcal M(\mathcal G,\mathcal F,\boldsymbol U)$ when it is a canonical SCM whose structural equations use the latent variables only as the $C^2$-components of $\mathcal G$ prescribe, with $f_i\in\mathcal F$; a bi-directed arrow of $\mathcal G$ permits shared latents but does not require them to be correlated, so the latent laws $P(U_C)$ may be arbitrary, degenerate laws included. → `CanonicalClassArbitraryLatentReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:448`)
- condition of the paper: A network in $\mathcal{NN}_{k_1,k_2}(W,L)$ has $L$ hidden layers, each of width at most $W$, followed by the affine output layer (width at most $W$ and exactly $W$ give the same class of functions: a narrower layer is padded with zero units). The covering number $\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,S,\|\cdot\|_1)$ is that of the cited source (Anthony and Bartlett): the least size of a subset $C\subseteq S$ such that every point of $S$ lies at $\ell_1$ distance strictly less than $\epsilon$ from some point of $C$. → `ReLUWidthCoveringOwnerReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:471`)
- condition of the paper: In the categorical structural equations, $\arg\max$ selects the smallest maximizing label when there is a tie. This is a measurable completion on ties; under the stipulated independent standard Gumbel noises and nonnegative propensity weights of positive total, it leaves every fixed-intervention law unchanged. → `CategoricalArgmaxTieSmallestLabel` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:275`)

**Taken from the literature: Yarotsky2018OptimalAO, Theorem 2.**

What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TbsNeuralCausalPi.YarotskyStructuralApprox` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/CitedResults.lean:140`)

```lean
def YarotskyStructuralApprox : Sort 0 :=
  ∀ d : ℕ, 0 < d → ∃ a c : ℝ, 0 < a ∧ 0 < c ∧
    ∀ (n b : ℕ) (kind : Fin n → NodeKind) (dims : Fin b → ℕ)
      (dag : Causalean.Graph.DAG (Fin n)) (G : SimpleGraph (Fin n))
      (C : Fin b → Set (Fin n)) (M : CanonicalSCM kind dims dag G C)
      (i : Fin n) (hd : structuralInputSize M.toEuclideanSCM i = d),
      let f := fun x : L1Vec d => M.f i
        (structuralFiniteCoordinates M.toEuclideanSCM i
          (WithLp.toLp 1 (fun k => x.ofLp (Fin.cast hd k))))
      ∀ L : ℝ≥0, LipschitzOnWith L f (unitCube d) →
        ∀ j : Fin (outputDim (kind i)),
          ∀ depth : ℕ, 1 ≤ depth → ∃ q : ReLUNet d 1 (2*d+10) depth,
            ∀ x ∈ unitCube d,
              |(f x).ofLp j - (q.eval x).ofLp 0| ≤
                a * euclidModulus (fun y => (f y).ofLp j)
                  (c * (depth : ℝ) ^ (-(2 : ℝ) / d))
```

The passage of the proof:

> By \cite[Theorem 2]{Yarotsky2018OptimalAO}, part b), applied with $p=2/d_{i}^{\text{in}}$ to each output coordinate of $f_i$, which is Lipschitz continuous on the input cube by \cref{assump:Lip_and_bounded}: there are narrow fully-connected ReLU networks of constant width $2d_{i}^{\text{in}}+10$ and depth $L_0$ whose sup-norm error is at most $a\,\omega(cW^{-2/d_{i}^{\text{in}}})$, where $W$, the number of weights, grows linearly in $L_0$ at fixed width; stacking the $d_{i}^{\text{out}}$ coordinates gives, if $V_i$ is continuous, $ \| \hat{f}_{i} -f_{i} \| _{\infty } \leqslant O(L_0^{-2/d_{i}^{\text{in}}}) \leqslant O(L_0^{-2/d_{\max}^{\text{in}}}) $.

## Proposition 4.1

**Statement (corrected).** \label{prop:counterexample} There exists a constant $c>0$ and an identifiable SCM $\mathcal{M}^*$ satisfying Assumptions \ref{assump:independence_u}-\ref{assumption:lower_bound} such that for any $\epsilon >0$, there exists an SCM $\mathcal{M}_\epsilon$ satisfying $W(P^{\mathcal{M}^*}(\boldsymbol{V}),P^{\mathcal{M}_\epsilon}(\boldsymbol{V})) \leq \epsilon $ and $|\text{ATE}_{\mathcal{M}^*} - \text{ATE}_{\mathcal{M}_\epsilon}| > c $. Here, for each non-Gumbel latent law $\mathbb P$ with connected support components $C_i$, Assumption~\ref{assumption:lower_bound} is required of each normalized component law $\mathbb P|_{C_i}/\mathbb P(C_i)$, with $K=C_i$.

**Lean.** `Papers.TbsNeuralCausalPi.counterexample_identifiable` — `lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/TCounterexample.lean:53`

**Status.** proved in Lean (the corrected statement), under 7 condition(s) made explicit. **Axioms.** the standard ones only (propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound).

A row marked ≈ was given these phrases with many rows of its result at once, not on its own.

Lean concludes what the paper's text does not state: `Integrable (fun u => backdoorOutcome (intervenedEval Mep.toEuclideanSCM (binaryAssignment true) u) - backdoorOutcome (intervenedEval Mep.toEuclideanSCM (binaryAssignment false) u)) Mep.toEuclideanSCM.latentLaw`.

**Conditions and readings, and the Lean definitions that carry them.**

- reading: $C$ is a $C^2$-component precisely when it is inclusion maximal among subsets of $\boldsymbol V$ whose distinct vertices are pairwise joined by a bidirected arrow. → `C2ComponentMaximalReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:58`)
- reading: Writing $x=(\text{Pa}(V_i),\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i})$, the displayed law means $P(V_i=k\mid x)=(f_i(x))_k/\|f_i(x)\|_1$ for the nonnegative weight vector defining the categorical distribution, with zero coordinates interpreted using $\log 0=-\infty$. → `CategoricalLawConditionalReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:400`)
- reading: For categorical $V_i$, $f_i(x)=(\mathbb{P}(V_i=j\mid x))_{j\in[n_i]}$ is the structural propensity vector, and $\|f_i(x)-f_i(x')\|\leqslant L_f\|x-x'\|$ on its structural input domain, using the paper's $1$-norm. → `PropensityLipschitzReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:408`)
- reading: There is a deterministic $K>0$ such that $\max_{i\in[n_V],j\in[n_U]}\{\|V_i\|_\infty,\|U_j\|_\infty\}\leqslant K$ almost surely under the SCM law. → `BoundednessAlmostSureReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:415`)
- reading: For distinct observed vertices $V_i,V_j$, the ``correlated latent variable pair'' criterion means that some $U_k\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i}$ and $U_l\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_j}$ are not independent, with $k=l$ permitted. → `BidirectedArrowReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:439`)
- reading: In the categorical branch of (\ref{eq:structure_equation}), zero coordinates of the normalized nonnegative vector $f_i(x)$ are allowed and are evaluated using $\log 0=-\infty$. → `CanonicalZeroPropensityReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:715`)
- reading: The second Bernoulli law in the construction has $P(U_2=0)=2/3$. → `CounterexampleSecondBernoulliReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Helpers/CounterexampleCanonical.lean:112`)
- definition of the paper: (Structural Causal Model) A Structural Causal Model (SCM) is a tuple $ \mathcal{M} =(\boldsymbol{V} ,\boldsymbol{U} , F ,P(\boldsymbol{U}),\mathcal{G}_0)$, where $ \boldsymbol{V} = \{V_i\}_{i=1}^{n_V}$ is the set of observed variables; $ \boldsymbol{U} = \{U_j\}_{j=1}^{n_U}$ is the set of latent variables; $ P(\boldsymbol{U})$ is the distribution of \ latent variables; $\mathcal{G}_0$ is an acyclic directed graph whose nodes are $\boldsymbol{V}$. The values of each observed variable $V_i$ are generated by \begin{equation} V_{i} =f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}) ,\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right) ,\quad \text{where} \ V_{i} \notin \text{Pa}( V_{i}) \ \text{and} \ \boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}} \subset \boldsymbol{U},\label{eq:structure_eq} \end{equation} where $ F = (f_{{1}} ,\cdots,f_{{n_{V}}})$, $\text{Pa}(V)$ is the set of parents of $V$ in graph $\mathcal{G}_0$ and $\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i}$ is the set of latent variables that affect $V_i$. $V_i$ takes either continuous values $\mathbb{R}^{d_i}$ or categorical in $[n_i]$. We extend graph $\mathcal{G}_0$ by adding bi-directed arrows between any $V_i,V_j\in \mathcal{G}_0$ if there exists a correlated latent variable pair $(U_k,U_l), U_k\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i}, U_l\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_j}$. We call the extended graph $\mathcal{G}_\mathcal{M}$ the causal graph of $\mathcal{M}$. → `StructuralSCM` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:157`)
- definition of the paper: (Average Treatment Effect). For SCM $ \mathcal{M}$, the ATE at $ \boldsymbol{T}=\boldsymbol{t}$ with respect to $ \boldsymbol{T}=\boldsymbol{t}_0$ is given by $\text{ATE}_{\mathcal{M}}(\boldsymbol{t}) =\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u} \sim P(\boldsymbol{U})}[ Y({\boldsymbol{t}}) -Y({\boldsymbol{t}}_{0})]$. → `ate` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:404`)
- definition of the paper: We let $ W(\cdot,\cdot) $ be the Wasserstein-1 distance and $S_\lambda(\mu,\nu)$ be the Sinkhorn distance \cite{chizat2020faster} with regularization parameter $\lambda > 0 $. → `wasserstein1` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:93`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assump:independence_u} All the latent variables in $ \boldsymbol{U}$ are independent. → `LatentBlocksIndependent` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:44`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assump:categorical_rv} The set of latent variables consists of two parts $\boldsymbol{U} =\{U_{1} ,\cdots ,U_{n_{U}} \}\cup \left\{G_{V_{i}} :V_{i} \ \text{is categorical}\right\}$. → `LatentGumbelDecomposition` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:52`)
- condition of the paper: Precisely, if $ V_{i} \in \boldsymbol{V}$ is a categorical variable, the data generation process of $ V_{i}$ satisfies $V_{i} =\arg\max_{k\in [ n_{i}]}\left\{g^{V_i}_{k} +\log\left( f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}),\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right)\right) _{k}\right\}\sim \text{Categorical}(f_i/\|f_i\|_1)$, where $ G_{V_i}= (g^{V_i}_1,\cdots,g^{V_i}_{n_i})$ are i.i.d. standard Gumbel variables, $\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i} \subset \{U_{1} ,\cdots ,U_{n_{U}} \}$. → `CategoricalGumbelArgmax` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:131`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assump:Lip_and_bounded} If $ V_i$ is continuous, $f_i$ in (\ref{eq:structure_equation}) are $L_f$-Lipschitz continuous. → `ContinuousMechanismsLipschitz` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:147`)
- condition of the paper: If $V_i$ is categorical, the propensity functions $f_i(\text{Pa}(V_i), \boldsymbol{U}_{V_i})\triangleq \mathbb{P}(V_i = j|\text{Pa}( V_{i}) ,\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}),j\in[n_i]$ are $L_f$-Lipschitz continuous. → `CategoricalPropensityLipschitz` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:174`)
- condition of the paper: There exists a constant $K>0$ such that $\max_{i\in [n_V],j\in [n_U]}\{\|V_i\|_\infty,\|U_j\|_\infty\} \leqslant K$. → `VariablesBounded` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:191`)
- condition of the paper: The support of measure $ \mathbb{P}$ has finite connected components $ C_{1} ,C_{2} \cdots C_{N_C} $, i.e., $ {\text{supp}(\mathbb{P})} =\bigcup _{i=1}^{N_C} C_{i}$ → `SupportFiniteComponents` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:28`)
- condition of the paper: each component $ C_{i}$ satisfies $\mathcal{H}([0,1]^{d^C_i},C_i) \cap \mathcal{F}_L([0,1]^{d^C_i},C_i) \neq \emptyset$ for some $d_i^C \geqslant 0$. → `ComponentsLipschitzHomeomorphic` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:36`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assumption:lower_bound} Suppose that $ \mathbb{P}$ is supported on a compact set $ K\subset \mathbb{R}^{D}$, there exists a constant $ C_{f} >0$, $ f\in \mathcal{H}([0,1]^{d},K) \cap \mathcal{F}_L([0,1]^{d},K)$, such that for any measurable set $ B\subset [ 0,1]^{d}$, $ \mathbb{P}\left( f( B)\right) \geqslant C_{f} \lambda ( B)$. → `LowerBoundDomination` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:59`)
- condition of the paper: Besides, if $d>0$, $(f^{-1})_{\#}\mathbb P$ vanishes on the boundary $\partial[0,1]^d$. → `PullbackVanishesOnBoundary` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:76`)
- definition of the paper: A SCM $ \mathcal{M}$ with causal graph $ \mathcal{G}$ has canonical form if \begin{enumerate} \item The set of latent variables consists of two sets, \begin{equation*} \boldsymbol{U} =\left\{U_{C} :C\ \text{is a} \ C^{2}\text{-component of} \ \mathcal{G}\right\} \cup \left\{G_{V_{i}} =( g^{V_{i}}_{1} ,\cdots ,g^{V_{i}}_{n_{i}}) :V_{i} \ \text{is categorical}\right\} , \end{equation*} where $ U_{C}$ and $ g_j^{V_{i}}$ are independent and $ g_j^{V_{i}}$ are standard Gumbel variables. \item The structure equations have the form \begin{equation}\label{eq:structure_equation} V_{i} =\begin{cases} f_{i}\left(\text{Pa} (V_{i} ),\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right) , & V_{i} \ \text{is continuous} ,\\ \arg\max_{k\in [ n_{i}]}\left\{g^{V_{i}}_{k} +\log\left( f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}) ,\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right)\right)_{k}\right\} , & V_{i} \ \text{is categorical} , \|f_i\|_1=1 , \end{cases} \end{equation} where $ \boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}} =\{U_{C} :V_{i} \in C, C \ \text{is a} \ C^{2}\text{-component of} \ \mathcal{G}\}$ and $(x)_k$ is the $k$-th coordinate of the vector $x$. We further assume that $f_i$ are normalized for categorical variables. \end{enumerate} Given a function class $\mathcal{F}$, the SCM class $\mathcal{M} (\mathcal{G} ,\mathcal{F} ,\boldsymbol{U} )$ consists of all canonical SCM models with causal graph $\mathcal{G}$ such that $ f_{i} \in \mathcal{F} ,i\in [ n_{V}]$. → `CanonicalSCM` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:606`)
- condition of the paper: A model belongs to $\mathcal M(\mathcal G,\mathcal F,\boldsymbol U)$ when it is a canonical SCM whose structural equations use the latent variables only as the $C^2$-components of $\mathcal G$ prescribe, with $f_i\in\mathcal F$; a bi-directed arrow of $\mathcal G$ permits shared latents but does not require them to be correlated, so the latent laws $P(U_C)$ may be arbitrary, degenerate laws included. → `CanonicalClassArbitraryLatentReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:448`)
- condition of the paper: Identifiable means: every SCM $\mathcal M$ on the backdoor graph of Proposition~\ref{prop:counterexample_foral} whose latent variables are all independent (Assumption~\ref{assump:independence_u}) and with $P^{\mathcal M}(\boldsymbol V)=P^{\mathcal M^*}(\boldsymbol V)$ has $\text{ATE}_{\mathcal M}=\text{ATE}_{\mathcal M^*}$; the comparison models $\mathcal M_\epsilon$ have that same graph. Models whose latents are pairwise independent but jointly dependent are not on the graph in this sense. → `IdentifiabilityIndependentLatentsReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/TCounterexample.lean:22`)
- condition of the paper: In the categorical structural equations, $\arg\max$ selects the smallest maximizing label when there is a tie. This is a measurable completion on ties; under the stipulated independent standard Gumbel noises and nonnegative propensity weights of positive total, it leaves every fixed-intervention law unchanged. → `CategoricalArgmaxTieSmallestLabel` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:275`)

## Theorem 4.2

**Statement (corrected).** Let $ \mathcal{M^*}$ be any SCM satisfying the assumptions of Corollary \ref{corollary:deep_nn}. Suppose that the Lipschitz constant of functions in $\mathcal{M}$ is $L_f$, $F:\mathbb{R}^{n_V}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}$ in (\ref{eq:opt_empiri}) is Lipschitz continuous and $\tau_n>0$, let $K>0$ be the constant in \cref{assump:Lip_and_bounded}, $\hat L_f=\sqrt{d_{\max}^{\text{in}}d_{\max}^{\text{out}}}L_f$, \begin{align*} \mathcal F_{0,n}&=\mathcal{NN}^{\hat L_f,K}\left(W_{0,n},\Theta\left(\log d_{\max}^{\text{in}}\right)\right),\qquad \mathcal F_{1,n}=\mathcal{NN}^{\infty,K}\left(\Theta(d_{\max}^U),L_{1,n},\Theta((d_{\max}^U)^2),L_{2,n},\tau_n\right), \end{align*} where $d_{\max}^U$, $d_{\max}^{\text{in}}$ and $d_{\max}^{\text{out}}$ are defined in Corollary \ref{corollary:deep_nn}. Suppose in addition that $d_{\max}^{\text{in}}\geqslant1$ and $d_{\max}^U\geqslant1$. In (\ref{eq:opt_empiri}), restrict each categorical raw structural network $q\in\mathcal F_{0,n}$ to satisfy $q(x)\in[0,\infty)^{n_i}$, $\|q(x)\|_1>0$ for every structural input $x$, and \[ \operatorname{Lip}\left(x\mapsto\frac{q(x)}{\|q(x)\|_1}\right)\leqslant\hat L_f. \] Put \[ D_U=\sum_{j=1}^{n_U}d_j^U,\qquad D=\max\{1,D_U\}, \] where the sum defining $D_U$ includes the non-Gumbel latent variables only. Take the radius to be $\alpha_n=\epsilon_n+s_n$, with positive tolerances chosen as sufficiently large constant multiples of the respective quantities in \[ \epsilon_n=O\left(W_{0,n}^{-1/d_{\max}^{\text{in}}}+\sum_{i=1}^2L_{i,n}^{-2/d_{\max}^U}+\tau_n(1-\log\tau_n)\right), \] \[ s_n=O\left(m_n^{-1/(D+2)}\log m_n+\frac{(L_{1,n}+L_{2,n})\log m_n}{\sqrt{m_n}}+n^{-1/\max\{2,D_U\}}\log^2 n+\log(nm_n)\lambda_n\right). \] For every continuous structural function $f_i$ of $\mathcal M^*$, suppose in addition that $\sup_x\|f_i(x)\|_1\leqslant K$ on its full structural input domain. Restrict every continuous structural network $\hat f_i\in\mathcal F_{0,n}$ in (\ref{eq:opt_empiri}) to satisfy this same uniform output $1$-norm bound. Retain the output bound in (\ref{eq:consistent_limiting}), namely \[ \mathcal F_L^K=\{f:\sup_x\|f(x)\|_1\leqslant K,\ \operatorname{Lip}(f)\leqslant L\}. \] Let $F_n$ be the optimal value of the empirical optimization with the objective and Sinkhorn constraint of (\ref{eq:opt_empiri}) and both the continuous and categorical restrictions just specified, and let $\underline F^{\hat L_f}$ be the optimal value of (\ref{eq:consistent_limiting}) with this bounded Lipschitz class. If $ m_n = \Omega(n), L_{i,n} = \Theta(m_{n}^{d_{\max}^{U} /\left( 2d_{\max}^{U} +4\right)}), i=1,2, \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty }\min\{\tau_n^{-1}, W_{0,n},(\log(nm_n)\lambda_n)^{-1}\}= \infty$ , then with probability 1, $ [\liminf _{n\rightarrow \infty } F_{n},\limsup _{n\rightarrow \infty } F_{n}] \subset [\underline{F}^{\hat{L}_f} ,F_{*}]$.
