Lean also declares what the paper leaves implicit (the objects and their types): `n b : ℕ`, `kind : Fin n → NodeKind`, `dims : Fin b → ℕ`, `dag : Causalean.Graph.DAG (Fin n)`, `G : SimpleGraph (Fin n)`, `C : Fin b → Set (Fin n)`, `Omega : Type*`, `MeasurableSpace Omega`, `mu : Measure Omega`, `IsProbabilityMeasure mu`, `M : CanonicalSCM kind dims dag G C`, `counts : Fin b → ℕ`, `sets : ∀ j, Fin (counts j) → Set (L1Vec (dims j))`, `dC : ∀ j, Fin (counts j) → ℕ`, `Lchart : ℝ≥0`, `treatment : Joint kind → ℝ`, `p : ParentSpace M.toEuclideanSCM T × ℝ → ℝ≥0∞`, `delta : ℝ`, `∀ c0 c1 c2 : ℕ`.

Lean assumes conditions that the paper's text does not state: `hm : ∀ r, 0 < m r`, `hlam : ∀ r, 0 < lam r`, `Joint kind`, `hPerekrestenko_of_gate : PerekrestenkoQuantizedGeneration`, `hYarotskyCoordinate_of_gate : YarotskyCoordinateApprox`, `hYarotskyStructural_of_gate : YarotskyStructuralApprox`, `hAnthonyBartlettPacking_of_gate : AnthonyBartlettPacking`, `hAnthonyBartlettSquaredLoss_of_gate : AnthonyBartlettSquaredLoss`, `hFournierGuillin_of_gate : FournierGuillinSmallDeviation`, `hY : valueDim (kind Y) = 1`, `hT : valueDim (kind T) = 1`, `htreatment : treatment = scalarOutcome T hT`, `htrueObjective : TrueObjectiveIntegrable M.toEuclideanSCM (singleLawIntervention T) (uniformIntervention t1 t2 ht12) (rateObjective (scalarOutcome Y hY))`, `hpopulationObjective : PopulationObjectiveIntegrable M K (LhatF M.toEuclideanSCM L) (singleLawIntervention T) (uniformIntervention t1 t2 ht12) (rateObjective (scalarOutcome Y hY))`, `0 < a0`, `0 < a1`, `0 < a2`, `a0 ≤ c0`, `a1 ≤ c1`, `a2 ≤ c2`, `hempiricalObjective : ∀ r, EmpiricalObjectiveIntegrable (G := G) (structuralClassN M K (LhatF M.toEuclideanSCM L) W0 c0 r) (generatorClassN counts dC K (maxLatentDim M.toEuclideanSCM) c1 c2 L1 L2 tau r) (restrictedCandidate K (LhatF M.toEuclideanSCM L)) (singleLawIntervention T) (uniformIntervention t1 t2 ht12) (rateObjective (scalarOutcome Y hY))`.

Lean concludes what the paper's text does not state: `∃ a0 a1 a2 : ℕ,`, `0 < a0`, `0 < a1`, `0 < a2`, `∃ hcert : ArchitectureConstructionThresholds M K (LhatF M.toEuclideanSCM L) counts dC a0 a1 a2,`.

**Conditions and readings, and the Lean definitions that carry them.**

- side condition: For every $k\in[n]$, $\int_{\mathbb R^d}\|x\|\,\mu_k(dx)+\int_{\mathbb R^d}\|x\|\,\hat\mu_k(dx)<\infty$. → `ComponentsFiniteFirstMoment` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Auxiliary.lean:108`)
- reading: For each of $\mathcal{M}$ and $\hat{\mathcal{M}}$, $\max_{i\in[n_V],j\in[n_U]}\{\|V_i\|_\infty,\|U_j\|_\infty\}\leqslant K$ almost surely, where the indexed $U_j$ are the non-Gumbel latent variables. → `BothModelsBoundedReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:423`)
- reading: $C$ is a $C^2$-component precisely when it is inclusion maximal among subsets of $\boldsymbol V$ whose distinct vertices are pairwise joined by a bidirected arrow. → `C2ComponentMaximalReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:58`)
- reading: Writing $x=(\text{Pa}(V_i),\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i})$, the displayed law means $P(V_i=k\mid x)=(f_i(x))_k/\|f_i(x)\|_1$ for the nonnegative weight vector defining the categorical distribution, with zero coordinates interpreted using $\log 0=-\infty$. → `CategoricalLawConditionalReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:400`)
- reading: For categorical $V_i$, $f_i(x)=(\mathbb{P}(V_i=j\mid x))_{j\in[n_i]}$ is the structural propensity vector, and $\|f_i(x)-f_i(x')\|\leqslant L_f\|x-x'\|$ on its structural input domain, using the paper's $1$-norm. → `PropensityLipschitzReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:408`)
- reading: There is a deterministic $K>0$ such that $\max_{i\in[n_V],j\in[n_U]}\{\|V_i\|_\infty,\|U_j\|_\infty\}\leqslant K$ almost surely under the SCM law. → `BoundednessAlmostSureReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:415`)
- reading: The continuous structural functions and categorical propensity functions of $\mathcal M^*$ have Lipschitz constants at most $L_f$ in the paper's $1$-norm. → `LipschitzRefersToTrueModel` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:354`)
- reading: The phrase ``uniform cubes'' denotes the unit cube domains $[0,1]^m$ used for deterministic uniform function approximation; it imposes no uniform probability law on structural inputs. → `UnitCubeDomainReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:430`)
- reading: The bound $K$ applies throughout the optimization class to the observed variables $V_i$ and indexed non-Gumbel latent variables $U_j$, with $n_i\leqslant K$ for categorical nodes and admissible intervention coordinates in $[-K,K]$; it does not bound the separately supplied standard Gumbel noises. → `OptimizationClassBoundScopeReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:680`)
- reading: The variable bound inherited from Assumption~\ref{assump:Lip_and_bounded} uses $\|\cdot\|_\infty$. → `RateClassBoundSupNormReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:701`)
- reading: For distinct observed vertices $V_i,V_j$, the ``correlated latent variable pair'' criterion means that some $U_k\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i}$ and $U_l\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_j}$ are not independent, with $k=l$ permitted. → `BidirectedArrowReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:439`)
- reading: In the categorical branch of (\ref{eq:structure_equation}), zero coordinates of the normalized nonnegative vector $f_i(x)$ are allowed and are evaluated using $\log 0=-\infty$. → `CanonicalZeroPropensityReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:715`)
- reading: Under $\boldsymbol{T}=\boldsymbol{t}$, every unreplaced equation is evaluated in topological order at the intervened values of its parents, with the original latent law unchanged; for canonical categorical nodes the unreplaced equation is the categorical branch of (\ref{eq:structure_equation}). → `InterventionRecursiveEvaluationReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:96`)
- reading: A network in $\mathcal{NN}_{k_1,k_2}(W,L)$ has $L$ hidden ReLU layers of width at most $W$, followed by an affine output layer of dimension $k_2$. → `ReLUArchitectureReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:462`)
- reading: Membership in $\text{NCM}_{\mathcal G}(\mathcal F_0,\mathcal F_1)$ requires $\hat f_i\in\mathcal F_0$ for every observed node and $\hat g_j\in\mathcal F_1$ for every latent component, with the compatible input and output dimensions understood separately for each network. → `NCMMembershipReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:521`)
- reading: Truncation means coordinatewise clipping of the final output to $[-K,K]$; in $\mathcal{NN}^{\infty,K}$ no finite Lipschitz bound is imposed. → `TruncationClippingReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:727`)
- reading: $\underline{F}^{L}$ denotes the constrained infimum of the displayed scalar objective over the stated canonical SCM class and its admissible latent laws, rather than a minimizing SCM. → `PopulationValueReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:535`)
- reading: $\mu_T=\text{Unif}([t_1,t_2])$ is the deterministic intervention probability measure. → `UniformInterventionReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:548`)
- reading: $d_{\max}^U=\max_{j\in[n_U]}d_j^U$ ranges over non-Gumbel latent variables, while $d_{\max}^{\mathrm{in}}=\max_i d_i^{\mathrm{in}}$ and $d_{\max}^{\mathrm{out}}=\max_i d_i^{\mathrm{out}}$, with $d_i^{\mathrm{out}}=n_i$ for categorical nodes. → `LatentDimMaxReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:560`)
- reading: The coordinates $P(U_j)$ in $\theta_{\mathcal M}$ are the population laws of the non-Gumbel latent variables, not necessarily uniform laws; the independent standard Gumbel laws are fixed and omitted from this population tuple. → `ParameterMarginalsReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:568`)
- reading: The gap event is $E_\delta=\{\exists\,1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n:|G_i+\log p_i-G_j-\log p_j|<\delta\}$, and its union bound counts each unordered pair once. → `GumbelGapEventReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:344`)
- reading: The measure $P$ is a probability measure on the treatment space, and for almost every parent value $x$, $\nu(dt\mid\text{Pa}(T)=x)=p_\nu(t\mid\text{Pa}(T)=x)P(dt)$, with the stated density version satisfying $p_\nu(t\mid\text{Pa}(T)=x)\geqslant\delta$ for every $t\in[t_1,t_2]$. → `TreatmentOverlapDensityReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:26`)
- reading: The functions $f,\hat{f}:[-K,K]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}$ are $L$-Lipschitz regression versions satisfying $f(T)=\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[Y\mid T]$ almost surely under $\nu$ and $\hat{f}(\hat{T})=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\hat{Y}\mid\hat{T}]$ almost surely under $\mu$. → `LipschitzRegressionBoxReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:37`)
- reading: The boundedness condition means $\|f(x)\|_{\infty}\leqslant K$ for every $f\in\mathcal{F}$ and $x\in\mathbb{R}^{d'}$. → `SupNormBoundReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:390`)
- reading: For each categorical $V_i$, the noise in equation (\ref{eq:structure_equation_hat}) satisfies $g_k=g_k^{V_i}$ for $k\in[n_i]$. → `NCMCategoricalNoiseReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:494`)
- reading: The layer uses $V=[\Vec{v}_1,\ldots,\Vec{v}_{N_C}]$ and the same Gumbel draws for all $\tau>0$, with $\lim_{\tau\downarrow0}\hat{X}^{\tau}_i=\hat{X}^{0}_i=\mathbb{I}_{\{i=\arg\max_{j\in[N_C]}(\log p_j+G_j)\}}$ almost surely for every $i\in[N_C]$. → `GumbelMixingLayerReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:505`)
- reading: $F_n=\inf\left\{\mathbb{E}_{t\sim\mu_T}\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\mathcal{M}}}[F(V_1(t),\ldots,V_{n_V}(t))]:\hat{\mathcal{M}}\in\text{NCM}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{F}_{0,n},\mathcal{F}_{1,n}),\ S_{\lambda_n}(P_{m_n}^{\hat{\mathcal{M}}}(\boldsymbol{V}),P_n^{\mathcal{M}^*}(\boldsymbol{V}))\leqslant\alpha_n\right\}$. → `EmpiricalValueInfimumReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:254`)
- reading: In the displayed softmax formula, $X_k$ means $X_k^\tau$, $\mu^\tau=P(X^\tau)$, and $X^0=\lim_{\tau\downarrow0}X^\tau$ almost surely with the same i.i.d. standard Gumbel variables $G_1,\ldots,G_n$ used for every $\tau$. → `GumbelSoftmaxSameDrawReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:578`)
- reading: $f\in\mathcal{H}(K_1,K_2)$ means that $f:K_1\rightarrow K_2$ is bijective and both $f$ and $f^{-1}:K_2\rightarrow K_1$ are continuous. → `HomeomorphismReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:455`)
- reading: For every $k\in[n]$, $\mu_k$ and $\hat\mu_k$ are probability measures on $\mathbb R^d$. → `MixtureComponentsProbability` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Auxiliary.lean:113`)
- reading: The law $\hat g_{\#}\lambda$ includes the independent standard Gumbel variables used in the mixing layer. → `GeneratorLawIncludesGumbel` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:73`)
- reading: The observational empirical law $P_n^{\mathcal M^*}(\boldsymbol V)$ is formed from $n$ independent complete observations with common law $P^{\mathcal M^*}(\boldsymbol V)$. → `ObservationalSampleIID` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:268`)
- reading: The empirical optimization ranges over network representations, including distinct representations with the same latent population laws. → `OptimizationOverRepresentations` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:217`)
- reading: The assertion about adjacent cubes is quantified over $i$ satisfying $1\leqslant i<2^{nd}$. → `HilbertAdjacencyRange` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:198`)
- reading: The measure $P$ is the reference probability measure on the treatment space: for $X=\text{Pa}(T)$, the conditional density notation means $\nu(dt|X=x)=p_\nu(t|X=x)P(dt)$. → `TreatmentReferenceDensity` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:239`)
- reading: The mass assigned to $I_{O_n(i)}$ is $\lambda_d(C_{O_n(i)})=2^{-nd}$. → `IntervalMassLebesgueReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:332`)
- reading: In the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:holder_curve}, $\mathcal C_n$ denotes the standard dyadic collection $\left\{\prod_{r=1}^d[a_r2^{-n},(a_r+1)2^{-n}]:a_r\in\{0,\ldots,2^n-1\}\right\}$. → `HolderCurveDyadicCellsReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:310`)
- reading: The common simulation draws are independent of the observational sample. → `SimulationIndependentOfSample` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:320`)
- reading: The common exogenous draws are independent and identically distributed according to the specified product law of the exogenous inputs. → `SimulationDrawsIID` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:312`)
- reading: The multiplicative width factors in $\Theta(d_{\max}^{\text{in}}d_{\max}^{\text{out}})$, $\Theta(d_{\max}^U)$, and $\Theta((d_{\max}^U)^2)$ can be chosen as absolute numerical constants, independent of the causal model, $R$, all dimensions, and all category and component counts. → `DeepNCMAbsoluteWidthReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:738`)
- reading: For each latent law $P(U_C)$, the assumptions of the proposition entitled ``Deep distribution approximation'' require the support charts of \cref{assump:support} and \cref{assumption:lower_bound} on each normalized component law $\mu_i=P(U_C)|_{C_i}/P(U_C\in C_i)$, with $(f^{-1})_{\#}\mu_i(\partial[0,1]^{d_i^C})=0$ when $d_i^C>0$. → `DeepNNLatentLawPremisesReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:750`)
- reading: Probabilities of uniform deviation events are evaluated in the completion of the sample law. → `SampleLawCompletionProbability` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:44`)
- reading: The structural uniform infinity norm takes the supremum of the coordinatewise infinity norm over the common structural input domain. → `StructuralSupNormConvention` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:205`)
- reading: For each component of positive dimension $q=d_i^C$, the application of Theorem VIII.1 of \cite{perekrestenkoHighDimensionalDistributionGeneration2022a} uses integer depth parameter $s=1$, quantization parameter $\delta=1/\lceil\sqrt q(q+1)n(n-1)\rceil$, and quantization mesh $\delta/n$. → `PerekrestenkoParameterChoiceReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:594`)
- reading: For each $n$, every candidate is evaluated on the same $m_n$ draws of all its exogenous inputs, including its uniform inputs, generator Gumbel inputs, and categorical Gumbel inputs. → `CommonSimulationDraws` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:298`)
- reading: The error estimates are asymptotic as the network budgets tend to infinity and $\tau\downarrow0$, with constants independent of these budgets and of $\tau$. → `GeneratorBoundAsymptoticReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:85`)
- reading: The finite component counts and component cube dimensions of the latent generator architectures in $\mathcal F_{1,n}$ are fixed as $n$ varies. → `FixedGeneratorComponentStructure` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:300`)
- reading: The measures $\mu$ and $\hat\mu$ are probability measures. → `MeasuresAreProbability` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Auxiliary.lean:18`)
- reading: In Corollary~\ref{corollary:deep_nn}, for $m_i=d_i^{\text{in}}>0$, the symbol $\hat f_i$ denotes $q_i\circ\Pi_i$ for a continuous variable and the vector $\bigl(\max\{\epsilon_i,(q_i\circ\Pi_i)_k\}\bigr)_{k=1}^{n_i}$ for a categorical variable, where $q_i$, $\Pi_i$, and $\epsilon_i=A_iL_0^{-2/m_i}$ are chosen in its proof. → `ProjectedFlooredApproximantReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:630`)
- reading: The self terms in $S_\lambda(\mu,\nu)=W_{1,\lambda}(\mu,\nu)-\tfrac12W_{1,\lambda}(\mu,\mu)-\tfrac12W_{1,\lambda}(\nu,\nu)$ use the same $1$-norm ground distance on the corresponding support points. → `SinkhornSelfTermsGroundCost` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:137`)
- reading: In the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:holder_curve}, $\mathcal I_n$ denotes $\{I_{O_n(i)}:1\leqslant i\leqslant2^{nd}\}$ with $I_{O_n(i)}=[(i-1)2^{-nd},i2^{-nd}]$. → `HolderCurveIntervalsReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:324`)
- reading: The latent generator class in the statement uses the same temperature $\tau$ as the displayed error estimate. → `DeepNNSharedTemperatureReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:662`)
- reading: $F_n$ denotes the infimum of objective values, with an attained minimum having the same value. → `EmpiricalValueIsInfimum` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:236`)
- reading: For every $n$, the cells of $\mathcal C_n$ are closed coordinate boxes covering $[0,1]^d$; a cell may have zero side lengths. → `PartitionClosedBoxCellsReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:766`)
- reading: The observational empirical law $P_n^{\mathcal M^*}(\boldsymbol V)$ is formed from $n$ independent complete observations with common law $P^{\mathcal M^*}(\boldsymbol V)$. → `ObservationalSampleCompleteIID` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:289`)
- reading: The error estimate is asymptotic as $L_0,L_1,L_2\to\infty$ and $\tau\downarrow0$, for fixed $R$ and fixed causal model. → `DeepNNBoundAsymptoticReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:113`)
- reading: For a zero categorical propensity, $\log0=-\infty$. → `LogZeroConventionNCM` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:178`)
- reading: Ties in a categorical argmax are resolved by the smallest label. → `ArgmaxSmallestLabel` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:267`)
- reading: For distinct cells $C,C'\in\mathcal C_n$, the partition condition is $\operatorname{int}(C)\cap\operatorname{int}(C')=\emptyset$, with interiors taken in $\mathbb R^d$. → `PartitionInteriorsDisjointReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:775`)
- reading: A component with $d_i^C=0$ is a singleton, and its two deterministic blocks are the unique map to $[0,1]^0$ and a constant affine map to that singleton. → `SingletonComponentBlocks` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:126`)
- reading: The orders for $\epsilon_n$ and $s_n$ are asymptotic as $n\rightarrow\infty$. → `ToleranceOrdersAsymptotic` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:284`)
- reading: For a categorical propensity coordinate equal to zero, $\log0=-\infty$ in the canonical argmax construction. → `LogZeroConventionApprox` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:172`)
- reading: $W$ is Wasserstein-1 distance for the ground distance $\|x-y\|$. → `ProductWassersteinOneNormReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:52`)
- reading: In Corollary~\ref{corollary:deep_nn}, for $m_i=0$, the symbol $\hat f_i$ denotes the exact constant function $f_i$. → `ZeroInputExactApproximantReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:651`)
- reading: The architectural $\Theta$ orders use sufficiently large fixed constant factors supplied by the network constructions in the proof. → `ConstructionArchitectureConstantsReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Helpers/ArchitectureThresholds.lean:96`)
- reading: The refinement integer is $n=\lfloor(W_1/A_q)^{1/q}\rfloor$ for $W_1\geqslant A_q2^q$, where $A_q\geqslant1$ depends only on $q$ and bounds the clipped network width by $A_qn^q$. → `PerekrestenkoRefinementIntegerReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:608`)
- reading: The reference measure $\lambda_d$ denotes Lebesgue measure on $[0,1]^d$. → `CubeLebesgueReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:317`)
- reading: Each $O_n$ is a bijection. → `HilbertOrderingBijective` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:191`)
- reading: $S_\lambda(\mu,\nu)=W_{1,\lambda}(\mu,\nu)-\tfrac12W_{1,\lambda}(\mu,\mu)-\tfrac12W_{1,\lambda}(\nu,\nu)$. → `SinkhornDebiasedReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:147`)
- reading: The probability bound is for each fixed pair $\delta,\epsilon>0$. → `SupWassersteinFixedTolerances` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:160`)
- reading: In the proposition entitled ``Deep distribution approximation'', the multiplicative width factors in $\Theta(D_C)$ and $\Theta(dD_C)$ are absolute numerical constants, independent of the measure, chart constants, dimensions, and component count. → `DeepGeneratorAbsoluteWidthReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:784`)
- reading: The cells of $\mathcal C_n$ are closed and may share boundaries. → `HilbertCellsClosed` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:184`)
- reading: The depth expression $\Theta(\log d_{\max}^{\text{in}})$ means $\Theta(1+\log d_{\max}^{\text{in}})$ at $d_{\max}^{\text{in}}=1$. → `DepthExpressionConvention` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:291`)
- reading: The uniform infinity norm is $\|F-\hat F\|_\infty=\sup_{x\in K}\max_{j\in[d_2]}|F_j(x)-\hat F_j(x)|$. → `SupNormConventionPushForward` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:66`)
- definition of the paper: \label{cor:rate} Let $F, \mu_T$ in \cref{thm:consistency} to be $ F(\boldsymbol{V}) = Y, \mu_T \sim \text{Unif}([t_1, t_2]), \epsilon >0 $. → `rateObjectiveSetup` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Optimization.lean:124`)
- definition of the paper: In the finite sample setting, we consider the following estimator $F_n$ of the optimal values of (\ref{prob:pid}). \begin{align} F_n = \argmin_{\hat{\mathcal{M}} \in \text{NCM}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{F}_{0,n} ,\mathcal{F}_{1,n} )} & \mathbb{E}_{t\sim \mu _{T}}\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\mathcal{M}}}[ F( V_{1}( t) ,\cdots ,V_{n_{V}}( t))] ,\label{eq:opt_empiri}\\ s.t.~~~ & S_{\lambda_n}(P_{m_n}^{\hat{\mathcal{M}}}(\boldsymbol{V}) ,P^{\mathcal{M}^*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{V}) )\leqslant \alpha _{n} , \notag \end{align} where $ P^{\mathcal{M}^*}_{n}, P_{m_n}^{\hat{\mathcal{M}}} $ are the empirical distribution of $ P^{\mathcal{M}^*},P^{\hat{\mathcal{M}}}$ with sample size $n,m_n$, $\mu_T$ is a given measure, $S_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot)$ is the Sinkhorn distance and $\mathcal{F}_{i,n}$ will be specified later. → `empiricalPIValue` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Optimization.lean:667`)
- definition of the paper: To state the theorem, we define $ F_{*} =\mathbb{E}_{t\sim \mu _{T}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{M}^*}[ F( V_{1}( t) ,\cdots ,V_{n_{V}}( t))]$ to be the true value → `trueObjective` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Optimization.lean:102`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assump:independence_u} All the latent variables in $ \boldsymbol{U}$ are independent. → `LatentBlocksIndependent` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:44`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assump:categorical_rv} The set of latent variables consists of two parts $\boldsymbol{U} =\{U_{1} ,\cdots ,U_{n_{U}} \}\cup \left\{G_{V_{i}} :V_{i} \ \text{is categorical}\right\}$. → `LatentGumbelDecomposition` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:52`)
- condition of the paper: Precisely, if $ V_{i} \in \boldsymbol{V}$ is a categorical variable, the data generation process of $ V_{i}$ satisfies $V_{i} =\arg\max_{k\in [ n_{i}]}\left\{g^{V_i}_{k} +\log\left( f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}),\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right)\right) _{k}\right\}\sim \text{Categorical}(f_i/\|f_i\|_1)$, where $ G_{V_i}= (g^{V_i}_1,\cdots,g^{V_i}_{n_i})$ are i.i.d. standard Gumbel variables, $\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i} \subset \{U_{1} ,\cdots ,U_{n_{U}} \}$. → `CategoricalGumbelArgmax` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:131`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assump:Lip_and_bounded} If $ V_i$ is continuous, $f_i$ in (\ref{eq:structure_equation}) are $L_f$-Lipschitz continuous. → `ContinuousMechanismsLipschitz` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:147`)
- condition of the paper: If $V_i$ is categorical, the propensity functions $f_i(\text{Pa}(V_i), \boldsymbol{U}_{V_i})\triangleq \mathbb{P}(V_i = j|\text{Pa}( V_{i}) ,\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}),j\in[n_i]$ are $L_f$-Lipschitz continuous. → `CategoricalPropensityLipschitz` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:174`)
- condition of the paper: There exists a constant $K>0$ such that $\max_{i\in [n_V],j\in [n_U]}\{\|V_i\|_\infty,\|U_j\|_\infty\} \leqslant K$. → `VariablesBounded` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:191`)
- condition of the paper: The support of measure $ \mathbb{P}$ has finite connected components $ C_{1} ,C_{2} \cdots C_{N_C} $, i.e., $ {\text{supp}(\mathbb{P})} =\bigcup _{i=1}^{N_C} C_{i}$ → `SupportFiniteComponents` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:28`)
- condition of the paper: each component $ C_{i}$ satisfies $\mathcal{H}([0,1]^{d^C_i},C_i) \cap \mathcal{F}_L([0,1]^{d^C_i},C_i) \neq \emptyset$ for some $d_i^C \geqslant 0$. → `ComponentsLipschitzHomeomorphic` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:36`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assumption:lower_bound} Suppose that $ \mathbb{P}$ is supported on a compact set $ K\subset \mathbb{R}^{D}$, there exists a constant $ C_{f} >0$, $ f\in \mathcal{H}([0,1]^{d},K) \cap \mathcal{F}_L([0,1]^{d},K)$, such that for any measurable set $ B\subset [ 0,1]^{d}$, $ \mathbb{P}\left( f( B)\right) \geqslant C_{f} \lambda ( B)$. → `LowerBoundDomination` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:59`)
- condition of the paper: Besides, if $d>0$, $(f^{-1})_{\#}\mathbb P$ vanishes on the boundary $\partial[0,1]^d$. → `PullbackVanishesOnBoundary` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:76`)
- condition of the paper: \label{thm:consistency} Let $ \mathcal{M^*}$ be any SCM satisfying the assumptions of Corollary \ref{corollary:deep_nn}. → `TrueSCMDeepNNPremises` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:79`)
- condition of the paper: Suppose that the Lipschitz constant of functions in $\mathcal{M}$ is $ L_f $ → `TrueStructuralLipschitz` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:95`)
- condition of the paper: $ F:\mathbb{R}^{n_{V}}\rightarrow \mathbb{R}$ in (\ref{eq:opt_empiri}) is Lipschitz continuous → `ObjectiveLipschitz` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:100`)
- condition of the paper: $\tau_n > 0 $ → `TemperaturesPositive` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:103`)
- condition of the paper: If $ m_n = \Omega(n), L_{i,n} = \Theta(m_{n}^{d_{\max}^{U} /\left( 2d_{\max}^{U} +4\right)}), i=1,2, \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty }\min\{\tau_n^{-1}, W_{0,n},(\log(nm_n)\lambda_n)^{-1}\}= \infty$ → `ConsistencyGrowthSchedule` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:115`)
- condition of the paper: Suppose the norms of all variables are bounded by $K>0$. → `AllVariablesNormBounded` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:204`)
- condition of the paper: If (1) (Overlap) $ \nu $ is absolutely continuous with respect to one probability measure $P$ and the density $ p_{\nu }\left( t|\text{Pa}( T) =x\right) \geqslant \delta >0 $ for x almost surely and $ t\in [ t_{1} ,t_{2}]$ → `TreatmentOverlap` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:255`)
- condition of the paper: (2) (No Confounding) there is no hidden confounder in the causal graph $\mathcal{G}$ → `NoHiddenConfounding` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:208`)
- condition of the paper: Suppose that the assumptions in Proposition \ref{prop:Lip_ATE} and \cref{thm:consistency} hold → `RatePremisesInherited` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:541`)
- definition of the paper: We denote $ \text{Lip}( f)$ to be the Lipschitz constant of a function $ f$ and define the truncated Lipschitz NN class, \begin{equation*} \mathcal{NN}_{d_{1} ,d_{2}}^{{L_f},{K}}(W,L) =\left\{\max\{-K,\min\{f,K\}\}:f\in \mathcal{NN}_{d_{1} ,d_{2}}\left({W} ,L\right),\text{Lip}(f) \leqslant L_f\right\}. \end{equation*} For simplicity, we omit the dimensions and use shorthand $ \mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}^{L_f,K}(W,L)$. Similarly, we also define the class $\mathcal{NN}^{\infty,K}(W_1,L_1,W_2,L_2,\tau)$ as a truncated version of $\mathcal{NN}(W_1,L_1,W_2,L_2,\tau)$. → `TruncatedLipNet` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Networks.lean:93`)
- definition of the paper: $ \underline{F}^{L}$ to be the optimal value of the following optimization problem over SCMs with Lipschitz constant $L$. \begin{align} \underline{F}^{L} = \argmin_{\hat{\mathcal{M}} \in \mathcal{M}\left(\mathcal{G} ,\mathcal{F}^K_{L} ,\boldsymbol{U}\right), P(\boldsymbol{U})} & \mathbb{E}_{t\sim \mu _{T}}\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\mathcal{M}}}[ F( V_{1}( t) ,\cdots ,V_{n_{V}}( t))] , \label{eq:consistent_limiting}\\ s.t. & W( P^{\hat{\mathcal{M}}}(\boldsymbol{V}) ,P^{\mathcal{M}^*}(\boldsymbol{V})) =0,\notag \end{align} where the minimum is taken over $\mathcal{M}\left(\mathcal{G} ,\mathcal{F}^K_{L} ,\boldsymbol{U}\right)$ with $\mathcal{F}^K_{L} = \{f: \|f\|\leqslant K, f \in \mathcal{F}_{L}\} $ and all latent distributions $P(\boldsymbol{U})$. → `populationLowerBound` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Optimization.lean:473`)
- definition of the paper: We use $ \| \cdotp \|, \|\cdot\|_\infty $ for the $1$-norm and $\infty$-norm and $[n]$ for $\{1,\cdots,n\}$. Bold letters represent sets of random variables. → `L1Vec` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:29`)
- definition of the paper: Let $ d_{\max}^{\text{in}} $ and $d_{\max}^{\text{out}}$ be the largest input and output dimension of $f_i$ in (\ref{eq:structure_equation}) and $d_{\max}^U$ be the largest dimension of all latent variables. → `maxInDim` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:814`)
- definition of the paper: A canonical causal model $ \mathcal{M} \in \mathcal{M}(\mathcal{G} ,\mathcal{F} ,\boldsymbol{U})$ is decided by \begin{equation*} \theta _{\mathcal{M}} =( f_{1} ,\cdots ,f_{n_{V}} ,{P}({U_{1}}) ,\cdots , {P}({U_{n_{U}}})) , \end{equation*} where $ f_{i}$ are functions in the structure equations (\ref{eq:structure_equation}) and $ U_{j}$ are uniform parts of the latent variables. We denote $ \mathcal{M}^{\theta }$ to be the SCM represented by $ \theta $, the underlying SCM be $ \mathcal{M}^{\theta^*}$ and $P^\theta$ to be the distribution of $ \mathcal{M}^\theta$. → `CanonicalParam` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Optimization.lean:177`)
- definition of the paper: We consider the space \begin{align*} M= \mathcal{F}_{V_1} &\times \cdots \times \mathcal{F}_{V_{n_V}} \times \mathcal{P}\left([ -K,K]^{d_{1}^{U}}\right) \cdots \times \mathcal{P}\left([ -K,K]^{d_{n_{U}}^{U}}\right) , \end{align*} → `PopulationParam` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Optimization.lean:406`)
- condition of the paper: In this result the bound $K$ applies to every SCM of the model class over which the optimization ranges, not only to $\mathcal M^*$: every variable has norm at most $K$, so each categorical $V_i$ with labels in $[n_i]$ has $n_i\leq K$, and every intervention value lies in $[-K,K]$. → `RateOptimizationClassBoundedByK` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:350`)
- condition of the paper: The expectation defining $F_*$ is a finite real iterated expectation. The inner objective is integrable under $P(\boldsymbol U)$ for $\mu_T$-almost every intervention value, and the resulting real conditional-expectation function is integrable under $\mu_T$. Almost-everywhere versions are allowed. → `TrueObjectiveIntegrable` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:29`)
- condition of the paper: Every objective expectation used in (\ref{eq:opt_empiri}) is a finite real iterated expectation. For each candidate over which the objective ranges, the inner objective is integrable under its latent law for $\mu_T$-almost every intervention value, and the resulting real conditional-expectation function is integrable under $\mu_T$. Almost-everywhere versions are allowed. → `EmpiricalObjectiveIntegrable` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:39`)
- condition of the paper: Every objective expectation used in (\ref{eq:consistent_limiting}) is a finite real iterated expectation. For each candidate over which the objective ranges, the inner objective is integrable under its latent law for $\mu_T$-almost every intervention value, and the resulting real conditional-expectation function is integrable under $\mu_T$. Almost-everywhere versions are allowed; one-sided infinite expectations are outside this real objective domain. → `PopulationObjectiveIntegrable` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Estimation.lean:53`)
- condition of the paper: In the categorical structural equations, $\arg\max$ selects the smallest maximizing label when there is a tie. This is a measurable completion on ties; under the stipulated independent standard Gumbel noises and nonnegative propensity weights of positive total, it leaves every fixed-intervention law unchanged. → `CategoricalArgmaxTieSmallestLabel` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:275`)

## Proposition B.1

**Statement (corrected).** Suppose that \cref{assump:independence_u} and \cref{assump:categorical_rv} hold, given any SCM $\mathcal{M} $ with causal graph $\mathcal{G}$ and latent variables $\boldsymbol{U}$, we can construct a canonical SCM $\hat{\mathcal{M}}$ of the form (\ref{eq:structure_equation}) by merging the latent variables in $\boldsymbol{U}$ such that $\mathcal{M}$ and $\hat{\mathcal{M}}$ produce the same intervention results. Besides, the continuous structural functions in $\hat{\mathcal M}$ have the same smoothness as those in $\mathcal M$. The categorical functions in $\hat{\mathcal M}$ have the smoothness of the normalized propensity functions $f_i/\|f_i\|_1$: normalization preserves differentiability order and local Lipschitz regularity wherever $\|f_i\|_1>0$, and coordinate regrouping preserves any global Lipschitz regularity these normalized functions possess.

**Lean.** `Papers.TbsNeuralCausalPi.exists_canonical_representation` — `lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/TEquivalentGConstrianed.lean:39`

**Status.** proved in Lean (the corrected statement), under 7 condition(s) made explicit. **Axioms.** the standard ones only (propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound).

Lean also declares what the paper leaves implicit (the objects and their types): `n b : ℕ`, `kind : Fin n → NodeKind`, `dims : Fin b → ℕ`.

**Conditions and readings, and the Lean definitions that carry them.**

- reading: $C$ is a $C^2$-component precisely when it is inclusion maximal among subsets of $\boldsymbol V$ whose distinct vertices are pairwise joined by a bidirected arrow. → `C2ComponentMaximalReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:58`)
- reading: Writing $x=(\text{Pa}(V_i),\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i})$, the displayed law means $P(V_i=k\mid x)=(f_i(x))_k/\|f_i(x)\|_1$ for the nonnegative weight vector defining the categorical distribution, with zero coordinates interpreted using $\log 0=-\infty$. → `CategoricalLawConditionalReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:400`)
- reading: For distinct observed vertices $V_i,V_j$, the ``correlated latent variable pair'' criterion means that some $U_k\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i}$ and $U_l\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_j}$ are not independent, with $k=l$ permitted. → `BidirectedArrowReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:439`)
- reading: In the categorical branch of (\ref{eq:structure_equation}), zero coordinates of the normalized nonnegative vector $f_i(x)$ are allowed and are evaluated using $\log 0=-\infty$. → `CanonicalZeroPropensityReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:715`)
- reading: Under $\boldsymbol{T}=\boldsymbol{t}$, every unreplaced equation is evaluated in topological order at the intervened values of its parents, with the original latent law unchanged; for canonical categorical nodes the unreplaced equation is the categorical branch of (\ref{eq:structure_equation}). → `InterventionRecursiveEvaluationReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:96`)
- reading: The categorical generation formula requires $f_i(x)\in[0,\infty)^{n_i}$ and $\|f_i(x)\|_1>0$ on its structural input domain. → `CategoricalWeightsPositive` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:74`)
- reading: A zero categorical coordinate has extended logarithm $\log0=-\infty$. → `LogZeroConventionCanonical` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:166`)
- definition of the paper: (Structural Causal Model) A Structural Causal Model (SCM) is a tuple $ \mathcal{M} =(\boldsymbol{V} ,\boldsymbol{U} , F ,P(\boldsymbol{U}),\mathcal{G}_0)$, where $ \boldsymbol{V} = \{V_i\}_{i=1}^{n_V}$ is the set of observed variables; $ \boldsymbol{U} = \{U_j\}_{j=1}^{n_U}$ is the set of latent variables; $ P(\boldsymbol{U})$ is the distribution of \ latent variables; $\mathcal{G}_0$ is an acyclic directed graph whose nodes are $\boldsymbol{V}$. The values of each observed variable $V_i$ are generated by \begin{equation} V_{i} =f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}) ,\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right) ,\quad \text{where} \ V_{i} \notin \text{Pa}( V_{i}) \ \text{and} \ \boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}} \subset \boldsymbol{U},\label{eq:structure_eq} \end{equation} where $ F = (f_{{1}} ,\cdots,f_{{n_{V}}})$, $\text{Pa}(V)$ is the set of parents of $V$ in graph $\mathcal{G}_0$ and $\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i}$ is the set of latent variables that affect $V_i$. $V_i$ takes either continuous values $\mathbb{R}^{d_i}$ or categorical in $[n_i]$. We extend graph $\mathcal{G}_0$ by adding bi-directed arrows between any $V_i,V_j\in \mathcal{G}_0$ if there exists a correlated latent variable pair $(U_k,U_l), U_k\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i}, U_l\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_j}$. We call the extended graph $\mathcal{G}_\mathcal{M}$ the causal graph of $\mathcal{M}$. → `StructuralSCM` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:157`)
- definition of the paper: A SCM $ \mathcal{M}$ with causal graph $ \mathcal{G}$ has canonical form if \begin{enumerate} \item The set of latent variables consists of two sets, \begin{equation*} \boldsymbol{U} =\left\{U_{C} :C\ \text{is a} \ C^{2}\text{-component of} \ \mathcal{G}\right\} \cup \left\{G_{V_{i}} =( g^{V_{i}}_{1} ,\cdots ,g^{V_{i}}_{n_{i}}) :V_{i} \ \text{is categorical}\right\} , \end{equation*} where $ U_{C}$ and $ g_j^{V_{i}}$ are independent and $ g_j^{V_{i}}$ are standard Gumbel variables. \item The structure equations have the form \begin{equation}\label{eq:structure_equation} V_{i} =\begin{cases} f_{i}\left(\text{Pa} (V_{i} ),\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right) , & V_{i} \ \text{is continuous} ,\\ \arg\max_{k\in [ n_{i}]}\left\{g^{V_{i}}_{k} +\log\left( f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}) ,\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right)\right)_{k}\right\} , & V_{i} \ \text{is categorical} , \|f_i\|_1=1 , \end{cases} \end{equation} where $ \boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}} =\{U_{C} :V_{i} \in C, C \ \text{is a} \ C^{2}\text{-component of} \ \mathcal{G}\}$ and $(x)_k$ is the $k$-th coordinate of the vector $x$. We further assume that $f_i$ are normalized for categorical variables. \end{enumerate} Given a function class $\mathcal{F}$, the SCM class $\mathcal{M} (\mathcal{G} ,\mathcal{F} ,\boldsymbol{U} )$ consists of all canonical SCM models with causal graph $\mathcal{G}$ such that $ f_{i} \in \mathcal{F} ,i\in [ n_{V}]$. → `CanonicalSCM` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:606`)
- definition of the paper: For a causal graph $ \mathcal{G}$, a subset $ C\subset \boldsymbol{V}$ is $ C^{2}$-component if each pair $ V_{i} ,V_{j} \in C$ is connected by a bi-directed arrow in $\mathcal{G}$ and $ C$ is maximal. → `IsC2Component` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:413`)
- definition of the paper: Let $ \boldsymbol{T}\subset \boldsymbol{V}$ be a set of treatment variables. The goal is to estimate causal quantities under a given intervention $ \boldsymbol{T}=\boldsymbol{t}$. Formally, the structural equations of the intervened model are \begin{align*} & T_i = t_i, \quad \forall T_i\in\boldsymbol{T}, \\ & V_{i}(t) =f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}) ,\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right), \quad \forall V_i \notin \boldsymbol{T}. \end{align*} We denote $V_i(\boldsymbol{t})$ to be the value of $V_i$ under the intervention $\boldsymbol{T}=\boldsymbol{t}$ and $P^\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{V}(\boldsymbol{t}))$ to be the distribution of $\boldsymbol{V}(\boldsymbol{t})$ in $\mathcal{M}$. → `interventionalLaw` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:387`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assump:independence_u} All the latent variables in $ \boldsymbol{U}$ are independent. → `LatentBlocksIndependent` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:44`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assump:categorical_rv} The set of latent variables consists of two parts $\boldsymbol{U} =\{U_{1} ,\cdots ,U_{n_{U}} \}\cup \left\{G_{V_{i}} :V_{i} \ \text{is categorical}\right\}$. → `LatentGumbelDecomposition` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:52`)
- condition of the paper: Precisely, if $ V_{i} \in \boldsymbol{V}$ is a categorical variable, the data generation process of $ V_{i}$ satisfies $V_{i} =\arg\max_{k\in [ n_{i}]}\left\{g^{V_i}_{k} +\log\left( f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}),\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right)\right) _{k}\right\}\sim \text{Categorical}(f_i/\|f_i\|_1)$, where $ G_{V_i}= (g^{V_i}_1,\cdots,g^{V_i}_{n_i})$ are i.i.d. standard Gumbel variables, $\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i} \subset \{U_{1} ,\cdots ,U_{n_{U}} \}$. → `CategoricalGumbelArgmax` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:131`)
- definition of the paper: Let $\Delta_n = \{(p_1,\cdots,p_n):\sum_{i=1}^n p_i=1,p_i\geqslant 0\}$ be the probability simplex. → `probabilitySimplex` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:77`)
- condition of the paper: A model belongs to $\mathcal M(\mathcal G,\mathcal F,\boldsymbol U)$ when it is a canonical SCM whose structural equations use the latent variables only as the $C^2$-components of $\mathcal G$ prescribe, with $f_i\in\mathcal F$; a bi-directed arrow of $\mathcal G$ permits shared latents but does not require them to be correlated, so the latent laws $P(U_C)$ may be arbitrary, degenerate laws included. → `CanonicalClassArbitraryLatentReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:448`)
- condition of the paper: In the categorical structural equations, $\arg\max$ selects the smallest maximizing label when there is a tie. This is a measurable completion on ties; under the stipulated independent standard Gumbel noises and nonnegative propensity weights of positive total, it leaves every fixed-intervention law unchanged. → `CategoricalArgmaxTieSmallestLabel` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:275`)

## Proposition B.2

**Statement.** \label{prop:holder_curve} Let $ \lambda $ be the Lebesgue measure on $ [ 0,1]$. Given any probability measure $ \mathbb{P}$ that satisfies \cref{assumption:lower_bound}, there exists a continuous curve $ \gamma : [0,1]\rightarrow \text{supp}(\mathbb{P})$ such that $\gamma _{\#} \lambda =\mathbb{P}$. Furthermore, if $d \geqslant 1$, $\gamma$ is $ 1/d$-Hölder continuous.

**Lean.** `Papers.TbsNeuralCausalPi.exists_measure_representing_holder_curve` — `lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Helpers/HilbertCurve.lean:386`

**Status.** proved in Lean, under 10 condition(s) made explicit. **Axioms.** the standard ones only (propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound).

- definition of the paper: Given a measure $ \mu $ on $ \mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}$ and a measurable function $ f:\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\rightarrow \mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}$, the push-forward measure $ f_{\#} \mu $ is defined as $ f_{\#} \mu ( B) =\mu \left( f^{-1}( B)\right)$ for all measurable sets $ B$. → `pushForward` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:72`)
- definition of the paper: Let $ \{\mathcal{C}_{n}\}$ be a sequence of partitions of the unit cube $ [ 0,1]^{d}$. We say $ \{\mathcal{C}_{n}\}$ has property (*) if the following properties are satisfied. \begin{enumerate} \item $ \mathcal{C}_{0} =\{C_{-1}\} ,C_{-1} =[ 0,1]^{d}$ \item $ \mathcal{C}_{n+1} =\{C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n+1}}\}_{i_{j} =0,\cdots ,2^{d} -1}$ is a refinement of $ \mathcal{C}_{n} =\{C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n}}\}_{i_{j} =0,\cdots ,2^{d} -1}$, i.e., $ C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n}} =\bigcup _{i_{n+1} =0}^{2^{d} -1} C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n+1}}$. Besides, $ \mathcal{C}_n$ is obtained by cutting the unit cube $[0,1]^d$ by planes that are paralleled to the coordinate planes. \end{enumerate} → `HasPropertyStar` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Cubes.lean:44`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assumption:lower_bound} Suppose that $ \mathbb{P}$ is supported on a compact set $ K\subset \mathbb{R}^{D}$, there exists a constant $ C_{f} >0$, $ f\in \mathcal{H}([0,1]^{d},K) \cap \mathcal{F}_L([0,1]^{d},K)$, such that for any measurable set $ B\subset [ 0,1]^{d}$, $ \mathbb{P}\left( f( B)\right) \geqslant C_{f} \lambda ( B)$. → `LowerBoundDomination` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:59`)
- condition of the paper: Besides, if $d>0$, $(f^{-1})_{\#}\mathbb P$ vanishes on the boundary $\partial[0,1]^d$. → `PullbackVanishesOnBoundary` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:76`)
- definition of the paper: For a $ n$-dimensional cube $ C=\prod _{i=1}^{n}[ a_{i} ,b_{i}]$, the diameter of $ C$ is $ \text{diam}( C) =\sqrt{\sum _{i=1}^{n}( b_{i} -a_{i})^{2}}$. We also define the function $ R$ that measures the ratio between maximum edge and minimum edge. For a collection of cubes $ \mathcal{C} =\{C_{1} ,\cdots ,C_{m}\} ,C_{k} =\prod _{i=1}^{n}\left[ a_{i}^{k} ,b_{i}^{k}\right]$, $ R(\mathcal{C})$ is defined to be \begin{equation*} R(\mathcal{C}) =\frac{\max_{i,k} |a_{i}^{k} -b_{i}^{k} |}{\min_{i,k} |a_{i}^{k} -b_{i}^{k} |} . \end{equation*} → `cubeMetrics` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Cubes.lean:32`)
- definition of the paper: First, we construct a sequence of partitions that has the property (*) recursively. Let $ \mathcal{C}_{0} =\left\{[ 0,1]^{d}\right\}$. Suppose we have defined close cubes collection $ \mathcal{C}_{n} =\{C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n}}\}_{i_{j} =0,\cdots ,2^{d} -1}$, , such that \begin{enumerate} \item $ \mathcal{C}_n$ is obtained by cutting the unit cube $[0,1]^d$ by planes that are paralleled to the coordinate planes. \item $ C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n-1}} =\bigcup _{i_{n}} C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n}}$ and $ \text{diam}( C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n-1}}) \leqslant 2/3\cdot \text{diam}( C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n}})$. \item $ \mathbb{P}( \partial C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n}}) =0$ and $ R(\mathcal{C}_{n+1}) \leqslant \left( 1+2^{-n}\right) R(\mathcal{C}_{n})$. \end{enumerate} Next, we construct $ \mathcal{C}_{n+1}$ from $ \mathcal{C}_{n}$ that preserves these properties. We refine the division $ \mathcal{C}_{n} =\{C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n}}\}_{i_{j} =0,\cdots ,2^{d} -1}$ by union of hyperplane $ P_{i,r} =\{y:y_{i} =r\}$ to get $ \{C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n+1}}\}$ such that $ C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n}} =\bigcup _{i_{n+1}} C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n+1}}$ and $ \text{diam}( C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n-1}}) \leqslant 2/3\cdot \text{diam}( C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n}})$. By property 1, $ \mathcal{C}_{n}$ is obtained by dividing $ [ 0,1]^{d}$ using \begin{equation*} P_{1,r_{0}^{1}} ,\cdots ,P_{1,r_{2^{n} -1}^{1}} ,\cdots ,P_{i,r_{j}^{i}} ,\cdots ,P_{d,r_{2^{n}}^{d}} ,r_{0}^{i} =0,r_{2^{n}}^{i} =1, \end{equation*} we can further cut the unit cube using hyperplane $ \{P_{i,\left( r_{j}^{i} +r_{j+1}^{i}\right) /2)}\}_{i=1,\cdots d,j=1,\cdots 2^{n} -1}$. However, this construction may not satisfy property 3, $ \mathbb{P}( \partial C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n+1}}) =0$. We need to perturb each hyperplane to ensure a measure-zero boundary. Since \begin{equation*} Z_{i} =\left\{r\in \mathbb{R} :\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{x\in [ 0,1]^{d} ,[ x]_{i} =r \right\}\right) \neq 0\right\} \end{equation*}is at most countable, we can perturb the hyperplanes a little bit, i.e., $ \{P_{i,\left( r_{j}^{i} +r_{j+1}^{i}\right) /2+\epsilon _{i,j})}\}_{i=1,\cdots d,j=1,\cdots 2^{n} -1}$ to ensure property 3. In this way, we can choose $ |\epsilon _{i,j} |$ to be sufficiently small such that \begin{align*} \ \text{diam}( C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n+1}}) & \leqslant 2/3\cdot \text{diam}( C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n}}) , \end{align*}and \begin{equation*} \begin{aligned} \frac{\max_{i,j}\left(\left( r_{j+1}^{i} -r_{j}^{i}\right) /2+|\epsilon _{i,j} |\right)}{\min_{i,j}\left(\left( r_{j+1}^{i} -r_{j}^{i}\right) /2-|\epsilon _{i,j} |\right)} \leqslant & \frac{\max_{i,j}\left( r_{j+1}^{i} -r_{j}^{i}\right)}{\min_{i,j}\left( r_{j+1}^{i} -r_{j}^{i}\right)} \cdotp \left( 1+2^{-n}\right) \end{aligned} . \end{equation*} Therefore, $ R(\mathcal{C}_{n+1}) \leqslant \left( 1+2^{-n}\right) R(\mathcal{C}_{n})$ and it is easy to see that properties 1,2,3 are satisfied. → `dyadicCubeCollection` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Cubes.lean:53`)
- definition of the paper: Let $ \mathcal{I}_{0} =\{[ 0,1]\}$, we define $ \mathcal{I}_{n}$ recursively. Suppose we have define interval collection $ \mathcal{I}_{n} =\{\{I_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n}}\}_{i_{j} =0,\cdots ,2^{d} -1}\}$ such that $ I_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n-1}} =\bigcup _{i_{n}} I_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n}}$ and $ \mathbb{P}( C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n}}) =|I_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n}} |$. Since $ \mathbb{P}( \partial C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n+1}}) =0$, we have $ \mathbb{P}( C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n}}) =\sum _{i_{n+1}}\mathbb{P}( C_{i_{1} ,i_{2} \cdots ,i_{n+1}})$. With the ordering, we divide each $ I_{O_n(j)}, 0\leqslant j\leqslant 2^{nd} - 1 $ into $ 2^{d}$ closed sub-interval $ I_{O_{n+1}(i)}, j2^d \leqslant i < (j+1)2^d$ such that $I_{O_{n+1}(i)} = [\sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_k,\sum_{k=1}^{i} p_k]$ , where $p_i = \mathbb{P}(C_{O_{n+1}(i)})$. This construction satisfies $ |I_{O_{n+1}(i)}| = \mathbb{P}(C_{O_{n+1}(i)}) $. Note that by condition 3 in step 1, $\mathbb{P}$ vanishes at the boundary of the cubes. Therefore, \begin{equation} |I_{O_{n}(i)}| = \mathbb{P}(C_{O_{n}(i)}) = \sum_{j = (i-1)2^d + 1} ^{i2^d}\mathbb{P}(C_{O_{n+1}(j)})= \sum_{j = (i-1)2^d + 1} ^{i2^d} |I_{O_{n+1}(j)}|.\label{eq:sum_I} \end{equation} → `dyadicTimeCell` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Cubes.lean:61`)
- definition of the paper: \label{prop:holder_curve} Let $ \lambda $ be the Lebesgue measure on $ [ 0,1]$. → `curveLebesgue` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Cubes.lean:64`)

## Lemma B.3

**Statement (corrected).** Given a sequence of partitions of the unit cube $\{\mathcal C_n\}$ that has property (*), suppose that, when $d\geqslant1$, $\mathcal C_n$ is the standard dyadic grid of closed cubes \[ \mathcal C_n=\left\{\prod_{r=1}^d[a_r2^{-n},(a_r+1)2^{-n}]:a_r\in\{0,\ldots,2^n-1\}\right\}. \] There exists an ordering for each partition $\mathcal C_n$, i.e., \[ O_n:\{1,\ldots,2^{nd}\}\longrightarrow\{\overline{i_1\cdots i_n}:i_j=0,\ldots,2^d-1\}, \] such that the following two conclusions hold. \begin{enumerate} \item If $ ( j-1) \cdotp 2^{d} < i\leqslant j\cdotp 2^{d}$, the first $ n$ digits of $ O_{n+1}( i)$ are the same as $ O_{n}( j)$. \item Adjacent cubes in the ordering intersect, i.e., $ C_{O_{n}( i)} \cap C_{O_{n}( i+1)} \neq \emptyset $. Furthermore, $ C_{O_{n}( i)} \cap C_{O_{n}( i+1)}$ is a $d-1$ dimensional cube. \end{enumerate}

## Proposition C.1

**Statement (corrected).** \label{prop:counterexample_foral} There exists a constant $c>0$ and an SCM $\mathcal{M}^*$ satisfying Assumptions \ref{assump:independence_u}-\ref{assumption:lower_bound} with a backdoor causal graph such that there is no unobserved confounding in $\mathcal{M}^*$. For any $\epsilon >0$, there exists an SCM $\mathcal{M}_\epsilon$ with the same causal graph such that $W(P^{\mathcal{M}^*}(\boldsymbol{V}),P^{\mathcal{M}_\epsilon}(\boldsymbol{V})) \leq \epsilon $ and $|\text{ATE}_{\mathcal{M}^*} - \text{ATE}_{\mathcal{M}_\epsilon}| > c $. Here, for each non-Gumbel latent law $\mathbb P$ with connected support components $C_i$, Assumption~\ref{assumption:lower_bound} is required of each normalized component law $\mathbb P|_{C_i}/\mathbb P(C_i)$, with $K=C_i$.

**Lean.** `Papers.TbsNeuralCausalPi.counterexample_backdoor` — `lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/TCounterexampleForal.lean:60`

**Conditions and readings, and the Lean definitions that carry them.**

- reading: $C$ is a $C^2$-component precisely when it is inclusion maximal among subsets of $\boldsymbol V$ whose distinct vertices are pairwise joined by a bidirected arrow. → `C2ComponentMaximalReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:58`)
- reading: Writing $x=(\text{Pa}(V_i),\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i})$, the displayed law means $P(V_i=k\mid x)=(f_i(x))_k/\|f_i(x)\|_1$ for the nonnegative weight vector defining the categorical distribution, with zero coordinates interpreted using $\log 0=-\infty$. → `CategoricalLawConditionalReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:400`)
- reading: For categorical $V_i$, $f_i(x)=(\mathbb{P}(V_i=j\mid x))_{j\in[n_i]}$ is the structural propensity vector, and $\|f_i(x)-f_i(x')\|\leqslant L_f\|x-x'\|$ on its structural input domain, using the paper's $1$-norm. → `PropensityLipschitzReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:408`)
- reading: There is a deterministic $K>0$ such that $\max_{i\in[n_V],j\in[n_U]}\{\|V_i\|_\infty,\|U_j\|_\infty\}\leqslant K$ almost surely under the SCM law. → `BoundednessAlmostSureReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:415`)
- reading: For distinct observed vertices $V_i,V_j$, the ``correlated latent variable pair'' criterion means that some $U_k\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i}$ and $U_l\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_j}$ are not independent, with $k=l$ permitted. → `BidirectedArrowReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:439`)
- reading: In the categorical branch of (\ref{eq:structure_equation}), zero coordinates of the normalized nonnegative vector $f_i(x)$ are allowed and are evaluated using $\log 0=-\infty$. → `CanonicalZeroPropensityReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:715`)
- reading: The second Bernoulli law in the construction has $P(U_2=0)=2/3$. → `CounterexampleSecondBernoulliReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Helpers/CounterexampleCanonical.lean:112`)
- definition of the paper: (Structural Causal Model) A Structural Causal Model (SCM) is a tuple $ \mathcal{M} =(\boldsymbol{V} ,\boldsymbol{U} , F ,P(\boldsymbol{U}),\mathcal{G}_0)$, where $ \boldsymbol{V} = \{V_i\}_{i=1}^{n_V}$ is the set of observed variables; $ \boldsymbol{U} = \{U_j\}_{j=1}^{n_U}$ is the set of latent variables; $ P(\boldsymbol{U})$ is the distribution of \ latent variables; $\mathcal{G}_0$ is an acyclic directed graph whose nodes are $\boldsymbol{V}$. The values of each observed variable $V_i$ are generated by \begin{equation} V_{i} =f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}) ,\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right) ,\quad \text{where} \ V_{i} \notin \text{Pa}( V_{i}) \ \text{and} \ \boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}} \subset \boldsymbol{U},\label{eq:structure_eq} \end{equation} where $ F = (f_{{1}} ,\cdots,f_{{n_{V}}})$, $\text{Pa}(V)$ is the set of parents of $V$ in graph $\mathcal{G}_0$ and $\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i}$ is the set of latent variables that affect $V_i$. $V_i$ takes either continuous values $\mathbb{R}^{d_i}$ or categorical in $[n_i]$. We extend graph $\mathcal{G}_0$ by adding bi-directed arrows between any $V_i,V_j\in \mathcal{G}_0$ if there exists a correlated latent variable pair $(U_k,U_l), U_k\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i}, U_l\in\boldsymbol{U}_{V_j}$. We call the extended graph $\mathcal{G}_\mathcal{M}$ the causal graph of $\mathcal{M}$. → `StructuralSCM` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:157`)
- definition of the paper: A SCM $ \mathcal{M}$ with causal graph $ \mathcal{G}$ has canonical form if \begin{enumerate} \item The set of latent variables consists of two sets, \begin{equation*} \boldsymbol{U} =\left\{U_{C} :C\ \text{is a} \ C^{2}\text{-component of} \ \mathcal{G}\right\} \cup \left\{G_{V_{i}} =( g^{V_{i}}_{1} ,\cdots ,g^{V_{i}}_{n_{i}}) :V_{i} \ \text{is categorical}\right\} , \end{equation*} where $ U_{C}$ and $ g_j^{V_{i}}$ are independent and $ g_j^{V_{i}}$ are standard Gumbel variables. \item The structure equations have the form \begin{equation}\label{eq:structure_equation} V_{i} =\begin{cases} f_{i}\left(\text{Pa} (V_{i} ),\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right) , & V_{i} \ \text{is continuous} ,\\ \arg\max_{k\in [ n_{i}]}\left\{g^{V_{i}}_{k} +\log\left( f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}) ,\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right)\right)_{k}\right\} , & V_{i} \ \text{is categorical} , \|f_i\|_1=1 , \end{cases} \end{equation} where $ \boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}} =\{U_{C} :V_{i} \in C, C \ \text{is a} \ C^{2}\text{-component of} \ \mathcal{G}\}$ and $(x)_k$ is the $k$-th coordinate of the vector $x$. We further assume that $f_i$ are normalized for categorical variables. \end{enumerate} Given a function class $\mathcal{F}$, the SCM class $\mathcal{M} (\mathcal{G} ,\mathcal{F} ,\boldsymbol{U} )$ consists of all canonical SCM models with causal graph $\mathcal{G}$ such that $ f_{i} \in \mathcal{F} ,i\in [ n_{V}]$. → `CanonicalSCM` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:606`)
- definition of the paper: For a causal graph $ \mathcal{G}$, a subset $ C\subset \boldsymbol{V}$ is $ C^{2}$-component if each pair $ V_{i} ,V_{j} \in C$ is connected by a bi-directed arrow in $\mathcal{G}$ and $ C$ is maximal. → `IsC2Component` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:413`)
- definition of the paper: (Average Treatment Effect). For SCM $ \mathcal{M}$, the ATE at $ \boldsymbol{T}=\boldsymbol{t}$ with respect to $ \boldsymbol{T}=\boldsymbol{t}_0$ is given by $\text{ATE}_{\mathcal{M}}(\boldsymbol{t}) =\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{u} \sim P(\boldsymbol{U})}[ Y({\boldsymbol{t}}) -Y({\boldsymbol{t}}_{0})]$. → `ate` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/SCM.lean:404`)
- definition of the paper: We let $ W(\cdot,\cdot) $ be the Wasserstein-1 distance and $S_\lambda(\mu,\nu)$ be the Sinkhorn distance \cite{chizat2020faster} with regularization parameter $\lambda > 0 $. → `wasserstein1` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:93`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assump:independence_u} All the latent variables in $ \boldsymbol{U}$ are independent. → `LatentBlocksIndependent` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:44`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assump:categorical_rv} The set of latent variables consists of two parts $\boldsymbol{U} =\{U_{1} ,\cdots ,U_{n_{U}} \}\cup \left\{G_{V_{i}} :V_{i} \ \text{is categorical}\right\}$. → `LatentGumbelDecomposition` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:52`)
- condition of the paper: Precisely, if $ V_{i} \in \boldsymbol{V}$ is a categorical variable, the data generation process of $ V_{i}$ satisfies $V_{i} =\arg\max_{k\in [ n_{i}]}\left\{g^{V_i}_{k} +\log\left( f_{i}\left(\text{Pa}( V_{i}),\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}\right)\right) _{k}\right\}\sim \text{Categorical}(f_i/\|f_i\|_1)$, where $ G_{V_i}= (g^{V_i}_1,\cdots,g^{V_i}_{n_i})$ are i.i.d. standard Gumbel variables, $\boldsymbol{U}_{V_i} \subset \{U_{1} ,\cdots ,U_{n_{U}} \}$. → `CategoricalGumbelArgmax` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:131`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assump:Lip_and_bounded} If $ V_i$ is continuous, $f_i$ in (\ref{eq:structure_equation}) are $L_f$-Lipschitz continuous. → `ContinuousMechanismsLipschitz` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:147`)
- condition of the paper: If $V_i$ is categorical, the propensity functions $f_i(\text{Pa}(V_i), \boldsymbol{U}_{V_i})\triangleq \mathbb{P}(V_i = j|\text{Pa}( V_{i}) ,\boldsymbol{U}_{V_{i}}),j\in[n_i]$ are $L_f$-Lipschitz continuous. → `CategoricalPropensityLipschitz` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:174`)
- condition of the paper: There exists a constant $K>0$ such that $\max_{i\in [n_V],j\in [n_U]}\{\|V_i\|_\infty,\|U_j\|_\infty\} \leqslant K$. → `VariablesBounded` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Model.lean:191`)
- condition of the paper: The support of measure $ \mathbb{P}$ has finite connected components $ C_{1} ,C_{2} \cdots C_{N_C} $, i.e., $ {\text{supp}(\mathbb{P})} =\bigcup _{i=1}^{N_C} C_{i}$ → `SupportFiniteComponents` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:28`)
- condition of the paper: each component $ C_{i}$ satisfies $\mathcal{H}([0,1]^{d^C_i},C_i) \cap \mathcal{F}_L([0,1]^{d^C_i},C_i) \neq \emptyset$ for some $d_i^C \geqslant 0$. → `ComponentsLipschitzHomeomorphic` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:36`)
- condition of the paper: \label{assumption:lower_bound} Suppose that $ \mathbb{P}$ is supported on a compact set $ K\subset \mathbb{R}^{D}$, there exists a constant $ C_{f} >0$, $ f\in \mathcal{H}([0,1]^{d},K) \cap \mathcal{F}_L([0,1]^{d},K)$, such that for any measurable set $ B\subset [ 0,1]^{d}$, $ \mathbb{P}\left( f( B)\right) \geqslant C_{f} \lambda ( B)$. → `LowerBoundDomination` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:59`)
- condition of the paper: Besides, if $d>0$, $(f^{-1})_{\#}\mathbb P$ vanishes on the boundary $\partial[0,1]^d$. → `PullbackVanishesOnBoundary` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Generators.lean:76`)
- condition of the paper: A model belongs to $\mathcal M(\mathcal G,\mathcal F,\boldsymbol U)$ when it is a canonical SCM whose structural equations use the latent variables only as the $C^2$-components of $\mathcal G$ prescribe, with $f_i\in\mathcal F$; a bi-directed arrow of $\mathcal G$ permits shared latents but does not require them to be correlated, so the latent laws $P(U_C)$ may be arbitrary, degenerate laws included. → `CanonicalClassArbitraryLatentReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:448`)
- condition of the paper: In the categorical structural equations, $\arg\max$ selects the smallest maximizing label when there is a tie. This is a measurable completion on ties; under the stipulated independent standard Gumbel noises and nonnegative propensity weights of positive total, it leaves every fixed-intervention law unchanged. → `CategoricalArgmaxTieSmallestLabel` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:275`)

## Proposition C.2

**Statement.** \label{prop:convg_opt} Given a metric space $ (M,d) $ and sets $ \Theta_1\subset\Theta_2\cdots\subset\Theta_n\subset\cdots\subset \Theta_\infty \subset M$, where $\Theta_\infty = \overline{\cup_{n=1}^\infty \Theta_n}$ , positive sequences $ \{\epsilon_{n}\}_{n\in \mathbb{N}},\{\delta_{n}\}_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ such that $ \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty } \epsilon _{n}=\lim _{n\rightarrow \infty }\tau_n=\lim _{n\rightarrow \infty } \delta_{n} =0$ and continuous functions $ f,g_{n} :\Theta_\infty \rightarrow \mathbb{R}$, suppose that \begin{enumerate} \item $\Theta_\infty$ is compact. \item $g_n$ satisfies \begin{equation} \label{eq:aprox_lip} \|g_n(\theta_1) - g_n(\theta_2)\| \leqslant L_g d(\theta_1,\theta_2) + \tau_n, \quad \forall \theta_1,\theta_2\in\Theta \end{equation} and $ \sup_{\theta\in\Theta_\infty}\|g_{n}(\theta) - g(\theta)\|\leqslant \delta_n, g_n \geqslant 0$. \item There exists a compact subset $ \tilde{\Theta}_\infty \subset \Theta_\infty $ such that for any $\theta_0 \in \tilde{\Theta}_\infty$, there exists $\theta \in\Theta_n$ such that $ d(\theta,\theta_0 )\leqslant \epsilon_n.$ \end{enumerate} Consider the following optimization problems: \begin{align} \min_{\theta \in \Theta_n } & f( \theta ) , \notag\\ s.t. &\ g_{n}( \theta ) \leqslant L_g\epsilon_{n} + \delta_n, \label{eq:opt_n} \end{align} \begin{align} \min_{\theta \in \Theta_\infty } & f( \theta ) ,\notag\\ s.t. &\ g( \theta ) = 0. \label{eq:opt*} \end{align} and \begin{align} \min_{\theta \in \tilde{\Theta}_\infty } & f( \theta ) ,\notag\\ s.t. &\ g( \theta ) = 0. \label{eq:opt*_sub} \end{align} We assume that the feasible region of (\ref{eq:opt*}) and (\ref{eq:opt*_sub}) are nonempty. Let $ f_{n}^{*}, f^*, \tilde{f}^* $ be the minimal of (\ref{eq:opt_n}), (\ref{eq:opt*}) and (\ref{eq:opt*_sub}) respectively. Then, $ [\liminf _{k\rightarrow \infty } f_{n}^{*}, \limsup_{k\rightarrow \infty } f_{n}^{*}] \subset [f^{*}, \tilde{f}^*]$.

**Lean.** `Papers.TbsNeuralCausalPi.convgOpt_limits_mem` — `lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Helpers/ConvgOpt.lean:193`

## Lemma C.3

**Statement.** \label{lemma:Lip_condition} Let $ (\hat{T} ,\hat{Y}) \sim \mu ,( T,Y) \sim \nu $ and suppose that $ f( t) =\mathbb{E}_{\nu }[ Y|T] ,\hat{f}( t) =\mathbb{E}_{\mu }[\hat{Y} |\hat{T}]$ are $ L$-Lipschitz continuous and $|f(t)|\leqslant K, |\hat{f}(t)| \leqslant K$, then we have \begin{equation*} \int ( f( t) -\hat{f}( t))^{2} d\nu ( dt) \leqslant C_{W} W( \mu ,\nu ) , \end{equation*} where $ C_{W} =4LK+2K\max\{L,1\}$.

## Lemma E.4

**Statement.** \label{lemma:gumbel_err} Given $1 >\tau >0$ and $p_{1} ,\cdots ,p_{n} >0$, let $\mu ^{\tau } \sim X^{\tau } =\left( X_{1}^{\tau } ,\cdots ,X_{n}^{\tau }\right)$ be \begin{equation} X_{k} =\frac{\exp((\log p_{k} +G_{k}) /\tau )}{\sum _{k=1}^{n}\exp((\log p_{k} +G_{k}) /\tau )} ,\label{eq:gumbel} \end{equation} where $G_{k} \sim \exp( -x-\exp( -x))$ are i.i.d. standard Gumbel variables. Let $\mu ^{0}$ be the distribution of $X^{0} =\lim _{\tau \rightarrow 0} X^{\tau } =\left( X_{1}^{0} ,\cdots ,X_{n}^{0}\right)$. Then, \begin{equation*} W\left( \mu ^{\tau } ,\mu ^{0}\right) \leqslant 2( n-1) \tau -2n( n-1) e^{-1} \tau \log \tau . \end{equation*}

## Lemma E.5

**Statement (corrected).** For any $\mu\in\Delta_n$, $\nu\in\Delta_m$, and $\lambda>0$, let $\mu$ and $\nu$ be the weights of finite probability measures on points $x_1,\ldots,x_n$ and $y_1,\ldots,y_m$ in a common Euclidean space with the paper's $1$-norm. Put $c_{ij}=\|x_i-y_j\|$ and \[ \Pi(\mu,\nu)=\left\{T\in[0,\infty)^{n\times m}:\sum_jT_{ij}=\mu_i,\ \sum_iT_{ij}=\nu_j\right\},\qquad h(T)=1-\sum_{i,j}T_{ij}\log T_{ij}, \] with $0\log0=0$. Here the entropy regularized Wasserstein-1 distance is \[ W_{1,\lambda}(\mu,\nu)=\min_{T\in\Pi(\mu,\nu)}\left\{\sum_{i,j}c_{ij}T_{ij}-\lambda h(T)\right\}, \] and $W(\mu,\nu)=\min_{T\in\Pi(\mu,\nu)}\sum_{i,j}c_{ij}T_{ij}$. Then \begin{align*} -(\log(mn)+1)\lambda\leqslant W_{1,\lambda}(\mu,\nu)-W(\mu,\nu)\leqslant0. \end{align*} Moreover, we have the following estimation of approximation error. \begin{align*} |S_{\lambda }( \mu ,\nu ) -W( \mu ,\nu ) | & \leqslant 2(\log( mn) +1) \lambda . \end{align*}

**Lean.** `Papers.TbsNeuralCausalPi.sinkhorn_approx` — `lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Helpers/Sinkhorn.lean:164`

## Lemma E.6

**Statement (corrected).** Given a probability measure $\mu$ on $\mathbb R^{d'}$ and a class $\mathcal F$ of measurable functions $f:\mathbb R^{d'}\rightarrow\mathbb R^d$, suppose that each scalar coordinate class $\mathcal F_j=\{x\mapsto f_j(x):f\in\mathcal F\}$, $j\in[d]$, has pseudo-dimension less than $d_{\mathcal F}<\infty$ and there exists $K>0$ such that $\|f(x)\|_\infty\leqslant K$ for all $f\in\mathcal F$ and $x\in\mathbb R^{d'}$. Suppose in addition that there are a standard Borel space $T$ and a jointly Borel map $G:T\times\mathbb R^{d'}\rightarrow[-K,K]^d$ such that $\mathcal F=\{G(t,\cdot):t\in T\}$. There are positive constants $C,c$ depending only on $d,K$ such that, with probability at least \[ 1-\exp\left(C\left((1+\delta^{-d})\log(2+\delta^{-1})+d_{\mathcal F}\log(2+\epsilon^{-1})\right)-cn\epsilon^2\right), \] \begin{align*} \sup_{f\in\mathcal F}W(f_{\#}\mu,f_{\#}\mu_n)\leqslant\delta+\epsilon \end{align*} for all $\delta,\epsilon>0$, where $\mu_n$ is the empirical distribution of $\mu$.

**Conditions and readings, and the Lean definitions that carry them.**

- reading: The boundedness condition means $\|f(x)\|_{\infty}\leqslant K$ for every $f\in\mathcal{F}$ and $x\in\mathbb{R}^{d'}$. → `SupNormBoundReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:390`)
- reading: Probabilities of uniform deviation events are evaluated in the completion of the sample law. → `SampleLawCompletionProbability` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:44`)
- reading: The probability bound is for each fixed pair $\delta,\epsilon>0$. → `SupWassersteinFixedTolerances` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:160`)
- definition of the paper: Given a measure $ \mu $ on $ \mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}$ and a measurable function $ f:\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\rightarrow \mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}$, the push-forward measure $ f_{\#} \mu $ is defined as $ f_{\#} \mu ( B) =\mu \left( f^{-1}( B)\right)$ for all measurable sets $ B$. → `pushForward` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:72`)
- definition of the paper: We let $ W(\cdot,\cdot) $ be the Wasserstein-1 distance and $S_\lambda(\mu,\nu)$ be the Sinkhorn distance \cite{chizat2020faster} with regularization parameter $\lambda > 0 $. → `wasserstein1` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:93`)
- definition of the paper: We let $ \mathcal{F}_L(K_1,K_2)$ be the class of Lipschitz $L$-continuous functions $f:K_1\rightarrow K_2$. We may omit the domain and use $\mathcal{F}_L$ when the domain is clear from context. → `LipMap` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:82`)
- definition of the paper: We define \begin{align*} \mathcal{N}_{1}( \epsilon ,\mathcal{F} ,n) & =\sup _{x_{1} ,\cdots ,x_{n}}\mathcal{N}( \epsilon ,\{( f( x_{1}) ,\cdots ,f( x_{n}) :f\in \mathcal{F})\} ,\| \cdotp \| _{1}) , \end{align*}where $ \mathcal{N}( \epsilon ,S,\| \cdotp \| _{1})$ is the covering number of set $ S$ in $ \ell _{1}$ norm. → `empiricalL1CoveringNumber` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:171`)
- definition of the paper: We use $ \| \cdotp \|, \|\cdot\|_\infty $ for the $1$-norm and $\infty$-norm and $[n]$ for $\{1,\cdots,n\}$. Bold letters represent sets of random variables. → `L1Vec` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Notation.lean:29`)
- condition of the paper: A network in $\mathcal{NN}_{k_1,k_2}(W,L)$ has $L$ hidden layers, each of width at most $W$, followed by the affine output layer (width at most $W$ and exactly $W$ give the same class of functions: a narrower layer is padded with zero units). The covering number $\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,S,\|\cdot\|_1)$ is that of the cited source (Anthony and Bartlett): the least size of a subset $C\subseteq S$ such that every point of $S$ lies at $\ell_1$ distance strictly less than $\epsilon$ from some point of $C$. → `CoveringNumberOwnerReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/Assumptions/Conventions.lean:484`)

**Taken from the literature: anthony1999neural, Theorem 17.1.**

What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TbsNeuralCausalPi.AnthonyBartlettSquaredLoss` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/CitedResults.lean:183`)

```lean
def AnthonyBartlettSquaredLoss : Sort 0 :=
  ∀ (X : Type) (_mX : MeasurableSpace X) [StandardBorelSpace X]
    (G : Set (X → ℝ)), G.Nonempty → BorelParametrizedClass G →
    ∀ hG : (∀ g ∈ G, Measurable g ∧ ∀ x, g x ∈ Set.Icc (0:ℝ) 1),
    ∀ P : ProbabilityMeasure (X × Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1),
      ∀ (m : ℕ), 1 ≤ m → ∀ eps : ℝ, 0 < eps → eps < 1 →
        (Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin m => (P : Measure (X × Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1)))).completion
          {z : Fin m → X × Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1 | ∃ g ∈ G,
            |(∫ xy : X × Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1, (g xy.1 - xy.2.val)^2
                ∂(P : Measure (X × Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1))) -
              (m : ℝ)⁻¹ * ∑ i, (g (z i).1 - (z i).2.val)^2| ≥ eps} ≤
          4 * (N1avg (eps/16) G (2*m) (fun g hg => (hG g hg).2)).toENNReal *
            ENNReal.ofReal (Real.exp (-(eps^2 * m)/32))
```

The passage of the proof:

> The cited assertion used in Lemma~\ref{lemma:sup_wasserstein} is the following restricted form of Theorem 17.1 in \cite{anthony1999neural}, with its standing measurability condition. Let $X$ and $T$ be standard Borel spaces, let $g:T\times X\rightarrow[0,1]$ be jointly Borel, and put $\mathcal G=\{g(t,\cdot):t\in T\}\ne\varnothing$. For every Borel probability distribution $P$ on $X\times[0,1]$, every $0<\epsilon<1$ and every positive integer $m$,
> \[
> P^m\left\{\text{some }t\in T\text{ has }\left|\mathbb E_{(x,y)\sim P}(g(t,x)-y)^2-\frac1m\sum_{i=1}^m(g(t,x_i)-y_i)^2\right|\geqslant\epsilon\right\}
> \leqslant4\mathcal N_1^{\mathrm{avg}}(\epsilon/16,\mathcal G,2m)\exp(-\epsilon^2m/32).
> \]
> Here the sample consists of independent draws from $P$, the probability is taken in its completion, and $\mathcal N_1^{\mathrm{avg}}(r,\mathcal G,N)$ is the supremum over $x_1,\ldots,x_N\in X$ of the covering number of the evaluation vectors in the metric $N^{-1}\sum_i|u_i-v_i|$.

**Taken from the literature: anthony1999neural, Theorem 18.4.**

What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TbsNeuralCausalPi.AnthonyBartlettPacking` (`lean/Papers/TbsNeuralCausalPi/Setup/CitedResults.lean:164`)

```lean
def AnthonyBartlettPacking : Sort 0 :=
  ∀ (X : Type) (G : Set (X → ℝ)), G.Nonempty →
    ∀ hG : (∀ g ∈ G, ∀ x, g x ∈ Set.Icc (0:ℝ) 1),
    ∀ v : ℕ,
      Causalean.Stat.Concentration.HasPseudoDimAtMost (fun g : G => g.val) v →
      ∀ (m : ℕ), 1 ≤ m → ∀ eps : ℝ, 0 < eps → eps ≤ 1 →
        (M1avg eps G m hG).toENNReal <
          ENNReal.ofReal (Real.exp 1 * (v+1) * (2 * Real.exp 1 / eps) ^ v)
```

The passage of the proof:

> By Theorem 18.4 in \cite{anthony1999neural}, if $\mathcal G$ is a nonempty class of functions from a set $X$ into $[0,1]$ with finite pseudo-dimension $v$, then for every $0<\epsilon\leqslant1$ and every positive integer $m$,
> \begin{align*}
> \mathcal{N}_{1}^{\mathrm{avg}}(\epsilon,\mathcal G,m)\leqslant\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\mathrm{avg}}(\epsilon,\mathcal G,m)<e(v+1)\left(\frac{2e}{\epsilon}\right)^{v},
> \end{align*}
> where $\mathcal{N}_{1}^{\mathrm{avg}}(\epsilon,\mathcal G,m)$ and $\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\mathrm{avg}}(\epsilon,\mathcal G,m)$ are the largest, over $x_1,\ldots,x_m\in X$, covering and packing numbers of $\{(g(x_1),\ldots,g(x_m)):g\in\mathcal G\}$ in the averaged metric $d_1(u,w)=\frac1m\sum_{i=1}^m|u_i-w_i|$.
